Motivation: To address cross-domain generalization and reliability in mammography detection due to dataset heterogeneity; standard detectors degrade when trained/applied across diverse sources. MoE enables domain specialization and adaptive fusion, while calibration improves confidence reliability.

Method: Train multiple domain-specific expert models; employ a gating mechanism to adaptively weigh expert outputs based on input image; fuse outputs to enable domain-adaptive inference; introduce MoCAE calibration module to adjust confidence scores; evaluate on three public datasets (CSAW, DDSM, DMID) and compare against baseline detectors like YOLO/DETR in terms of average precision and calibration reliability.

Result: MammoMix achieves higher average precision and better reliability than baseline detectors, with gains more pronounced on datasets with greater variability; domain specialization and calibrated ensemble fusion contribute to superior generalization across heterogeneous mammography data.

Conclusion: Expert specialization via MoE and calibrated fusion significantly enhance robustness and generalization of lesion detection in cross-domain mammography, representing a promising approach toward dependable AI-assisted breast cancer screening across diverse clinical settings.

Abstract: Breast lesion detection in mammography remains a challenging task due to variations in image quality, lesion appearance, and population demographics across datasets. While current object detectors such as YOLO and DETR achieve strong results on individual datasets, their performance often degrades when trained on or applied across heterogeneous sources. To address this, we propose MammoMix, a novel framework based on Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) paradigm for robust and generalizable lesion detection. In MammoMix, each expert model is trained on a specific domain, allowing it to specialize in distinct characteristics of its source data. A gating mechanism adaptively weighs contributions from each expert based on input image, combining their outputs to enable domain-adaptive inference. To improve reliability, we further incorporate a calibration module, MoCAE, which adjusts confidence scores to reflect true predictive uncertainty. We evaluate MammoMix on 3 public mammography datasets: CSAW, DDSM, and DMID, covering diverse clinical settings. Results show that MammoMix outperforms baseline detectors in both average precision and reliability, particularly on datasets with greater variability. Our findings demonstrate that expert specialization and calibrated ensemble fusion significantly enhance model generalization and robustness. MammoMix offers a promising step toward dependable AI-assisted breast cancer screening across real-world clinical domains.

</details>


### [28] [Stream Forcing: Constructing Unified Training Trajectory for Robust Streaming Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10439)
*Yueting Zhu,Yuehao Song,Kaicheng Zhang,Bao Tang,Shaoyu Chen,Qian Zhang,Wenyu Liu,Xinggang Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Stream Forcing introduces a frame-indexed noise-level diffusion process for streaming video, with a continuous training trajectory from independent sampling to inference-consistent sampling, plus joint calibration and temporal correlative sampling, achieving substantial FVD gains on UCF-101 and robust long-horizon extrapolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addresses the train-inference mismatch in streaming video diffusion models where online inference follows a specialized denoising order, while training uses diverse noise configurations.

Method: Reformulate video diffusion sampling as a frame-indexed stochastic process over noise levels; construct a continuous training trajectory evolving from independent sampling to inference-consistent sampling; propose a joint calibration algorithm and a temporal correlative sampling algorithm to ensure trajectory smoothness and cross-frame correlation; unify these into the Stream Forcing training framework.

Result: Significant improvements in generation quality, notably 36.6% FVD improvement on UCF-101 and 27.9% FVD improvement for zero-shot long-horizon extrapolation on UCF-101.

Conclusion: Stream Forcing provides a balanced framework that enhances training sufficiency while maintaining inference efficiency, enabling better streaming video generation and long-horizon extrapolation.

Abstract: Streaming video generation holds strong potential for world modeling, where future frames must be inferred online sequentially to form a continuous video stream. However, streaming video diffusion models introduce a fundamental train-inference mismatch: inference follows a specialized denoising order, whereas advanced training strategies typically require diverse noise-level configurations. To address this trade-off between train-inference consistency and training coverage, we reformulate the video diffusion sampling as a frame-indexed stochastic process over noise levels. Within this stochastic process space, we construct a continuous training trajectory along which the sampling schedule progressively evolves from independent sampling to inference-consistent sampling. We further introduce a joint calibration algorithm and a temporal correlative sampling algorithm to ensure trajectory smoothness and cross-frame correlation. Building on these designs, we propose Stream Forcing, a unified training framework for streaming video generation that balances training sufficiency and inference efficiency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Stream Forcing significantly improves generation quality with a 36.6% FVD improvement on the UCF-101 benchmark. Furthermore, our method facilitates robust zero-shot extrapolation to long-horizon video generation with a 27.9% FVD improvement on the UCF-101 benchmark.

</details>


### [29] [FUSE: Frame-Unified Stress Estimation from Facial Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10442)
*Stefanos Gkikas,Thomas Kassiotis,Yang Guo,Guangliang Li,Giorgos Giannakakis*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FUSE introduces a frame-unified approach to facial-video stress detection by folding the temporal dimension into the channel dimension, enabling single-input inference over whole recordings without windowing. It uses a unified asymmetric-attention architecture and achieves competitive accuracy, with 69.44% test accuracy at temporal stride t=15 and 69.03% for full-frame input, across 58 subjects; computational cost ranges with stride (12.48–348.78 GFLOPs).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome limitations of temporal windowing in video-based stress detection, which complicates analysis and may miss long-range temporal cues; seek non-intrusive, end-to-end processing of full recordings.

Method: Convert the 120-second recording (3,600 frames at 30fps) into a single high-dimensional 2D representation by folding the temporal dimension into the channel dimension. Process with a unified asymmetric-attention architecture. Evaluate seven temporal-stride settings (from full-frame to sparse subsampling) on a 58-subject dataset with a subject-level protocol.

Result: Best test accuracy 69.44% at t=15, with full-frame (t=1) at 69.03% close to the best. GFLOPs vary from 12.48 to 348.78 across stride configurations, indicating a trade-off between temporal density and efficiency.

Conclusion: Temporal windowing is not essential for effective facial-video stress detection in this setting; complete-recording inference using a single unified architecture is feasible, offering a balance between accuracy and computational cost.

Abstract: Automatic stress detection from facial video offers a practical path to non-intrusive affect monitoring, yet existing video-based approaches commonly decompose full recordings into short temporal windows before classification. This design introduces additional choices regarding window length, overlap, and aggregation, while limiting direct analysis of temporal information across the entire recording. In this study, we present FUSE (Frame-Unified Stress Estimation), a facial-video stress detection framework that processes complete recordings as a single input without temporal windowing or external segmentation. The name reflects the defining operation of the method: rather than dividing a recording into short clips, all frames are fused into one unified two-dimensional representation from which the stress state is estimated. This unification is realized by folding the temporal dimension into the channel dimension of the spatial representation, and the resulting high-dimensional input is processed using a unified asymmetric-attention architecture. At a temporal stride of t = 1, FUSE retains the full 120-second recording as one input, corresponding to 3,600 frames at 30 fps. Experiments on a 58-subject stress dataset using a stratified subject-level protocol evaluate seven temporal-stride configurations, ranging from full-frame input to sparse subsampling. FUSE achieves the highest test accuracy of 69.44% at t = 15, while the full-frame configuration remains competitive at 69.03%. Across the stride range, computational cost varies from 12.48 to 348.78 GFLOPs, showing the trade-off between temporal density and efficiency. These results demonstrate that temporal windowing is not required for effective facial-video stress detection in this setting, and that complete-recording inference can be achieved within a single unified architecture.

</details>


### [30] [Bridging Event Streams and DiT: Event-Guided Video Frame Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10479)
*Guixu Lin,Yuyang Yu,Xiang Ji,Linyao Chen,Zhengwei Yin,Mengshun Hu,Mingdeng Cao,Shengfeng He,Yinqiang Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Adapter-based bridging of large temporal gaps in video frame interpolation using event-camera cues injected into a pre-trained image-to-video diffusion model; employs Image Warped Events (IWEs) and bidirectional sparse optical flow to guide generation, achieving superior fidelity and temporal coherence on real and synthetic data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Latent diffusion-based VFI struggles with large temporal gaps and complex motion, leading to motion blur and temporal wobbles. Event cameras provide high-temporal-resolution motion cues that can bridge gaps, motivating an adapter-based strategy to leverage these cues without retraining the diffusion model from scratch.

Method: Introduce an adapter-based framework that injects event-derived cues into a pre-trained image-to-video diffusion model. Use Image Warped Events (IWEs) and bidirectional sparse optical flow to provide spatially and temporally aligned guidance during diffusion, guiding both structure and motion in the generated frames.

Result: Experimental results on real and synthetic benchmarks show consistent improvements over existing state-of-the-art approaches, with reduced interpolation artifacts, higher reconstruction fidelity, and improved temporal coherence.

Conclusion: The approach demonstrates that minimal architectural modifications via adapters can effectively leverage event-derived cues to enhance diffusion-based VFI, enabling better handling of large temporal gaps and complex motion, and opening avenues for broader adoption and extension.

Abstract: Latent diffusion models have recently advanced video frame interpolation by synthesizing intermediate frames between input images. However, handling large temporal gaps and complex motion remains challenging, often resulting in motion blur, structural distortions, and temporal inconsistencies. Event cameras provide high-temporal-resolution motion cues that are well suited for bridging these gaps and improving interpolation quality. To exploit this advantage without training an event-assisted model from scratch, we propose an adapter-based framework that incorporates event-derived cues into a pre-trained image-to-video diffusion model with minimal architectural changes. Specifically, our method leverages Image Warped Events (IWEs) and bidirectional sparse optical flow to provide spatially and temporally aligned guidance during generation. By injecting these event-guided structural and motion cues into the diffusion process, our approach reduces interpolation artifacts and improves both reconstruction fidelity and temporal coherence. Experimental results on real and synthetic benchmarks show that our method consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art approaches. The project page is at https://joseph-lin-tech.github.io/BridgeEventDiT-VFI/.

</details>


### [31] [Precise Top-Layer Fabric Segmentation for Fabric Destacking with Edge- and Shape-Aware Deep Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10648)
*Wenbo Dong,Dipankar Bhattacharya,Akinari Kobayashi,Akira Seino,Fuyuki Tokuda,Xuzhao Huang,Kai Tang,Norman C. Tien,Kazuhiro Kosuge*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multi-branch segmentation training framework for top-layer fabric segmentation in stacked fabrics, combining an edge-aware branch for boundaries and a shape-aware branch aligned to CAD masks, achieving state-of-the-art results on a real fabric dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise segmentation of the top fabric layer in stacked fabrics is essential for robotic destacking but is difficult due to subtle boundaries and high visual similarity between layers; existing semantic/edge methods underperform and hinder manipulation tasks.

Method: Extend the encoder–decoder architecture with two auxiliary branches: an edge-aware branch to sharpen boundary delineation and a shape-aware branch to align the predicted fabric shape with CAD-derived reference masks; supervise the backbone with multi-branch losses; validate on a real-world fabric dataset and perform ablation studies.

Result: The multi-branch design outperforms established baselines on a real fabric dataset; ablation studies confirm the individual contributions of the edge-aware and shape-aware branches, with quantitative improvements reported.

Conclusion: A multi-branch segmentation training framework improves top-layer fabric segmentation in stacked fabrics, enabling better robotic destacking performance; future work may explore broader fabric types, real-time deployment, and integration with perception-action pipelines.

Abstract: Fabric destacking requires precise segmentation of the topmost fabric layer, a task complicated by subtle fabric boundaries and high visual similarity between fabric layers. Existing semantic and edge-based segmentation approaches often struggle with these complexities, limiting the performance of robotic manipulation for different tasks. In this work, a novel segmentation training architecture tailored for top-layer fabric segmentation in stacked fabrics is proposed. The method extends the classical encoder-decoder framework by introducing two specialized branches - an edge-aware branch and a shape-aware branch - that are used to supervise the backbone network for better tuning. The edge-aware branch enhances boundary delineation, while the shape-aware branch guides the network to capture and align the overall fabric shape with reference masks derived from Computer Aided Design (CAD) models. Experiments on a real-world fabric dataset demonstrate that the training approach outperforms established baselines, verifying the effectiveness of the multi-branch design through both quantitative results and ablation studies.

</details>


### [32] [When Vision Becomes Text: Visual Token Pruning via Cross-Modal Residual Guidance in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10489)
*Congyang Ou,Ruike Song,Yang Zhou,Libo Sun,Haokui Zhang,Zhenbo Luo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SIEVE: a training-free visual token pruning method for VLMs that uses Cross Modal Residual (CMR) and Cross Modal Absorption (CMA) to select a minimal set of tokens while preserving performance, achieving notable speedups on LLaVA-NeXT-7B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Massive visual tokens improve VLM perception but cause high inference costs. Existing pruning relies on local similarity signals and misses the full inference process as text tokens progressively absorb visual content across layers.

Method: Define CMA to quantify visual information absorbed by the text subspace; project visuals onto the text subspace using Tikhonov-regularized least squares and use residuals to measure irreducible visual information; combine CMR with text-attention relevance and residual-space diversity to select a training-free subset of tokens (SIEVE).

Result: Demonstrated across diverse VLMs; on LLaVA-NeXT-7B, retains 11.1% of visual tokens while preserving 97.5% of average performance, achieving 3.62x prefill speedup, 2.49x end-to-end speedup, and 6.02x KV-cache reduction.

Conclusion: Cross-modal absorption and residual-based pruning provide complementary guidance to similarity-based methods, enabling effective, training-free visual token compression with substantial efficiency gains.

Abstract: Abundant visual information strengthens vision-language model (VLM) perception, yet massive visual tokens raise inference costs. Existing visual token pruning methods rely on similarity-based guidance, which exploits pairwise text-vision and vision-vision token correlations for compression. However, such methods only capture local layer-level signals and overlook the whole inference process in VLM. In this paper, we revisit VLM inference and present a new efficient guidance scheme that complements similarity-based guidance. In particular, we identify a key observation: as LLM layers deepen, text tokens continuously aggregate visual information via self-attention and progressively absorb partial visual content into textual representations. To quantify this phenomenon, we propose Cross Modal Absorption (CMA) from a geometric representation perspective to measure how much visual information is absorbed by text, revealing that more visual tokens in deeper layers can be approximately explained by the text subspace. We accordingly propose Cross Modal Residual (CMR). It projects visual tokens onto the text subspace via Tikhonov regularized least squares and exploits reconstruction residuals to quantify visual information that cannot be explained by text. Finally, based on CMR, we present SIEVE, a training-free visual token compression method that combines CMR, text-attention relevance, and residual-space diversity to retain task-relevant and complementary tokens. Experiments on diverse VLM architectures verify the effectiveness of SIEVE. For instance, on LLaVA-NeXT-7B, SIEVE keeps only $11.1\%$ of visual tokens while preserving $97.5\%$ of the original average performance, achieving $3.62\times$ prefill speedup, $2.49\times$ end-to-end speedup, and a $6.02\times$ KV-cache reduction.

</details>


### [33] [GESTO: Human-Centric Spatio-Temporal Memory for Reasoning in Dynamic Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10886)
*Ermanno Bartoli,Buwei He,Dennis Rotondi,Sebastian Koch,Federico Tombari,Kai O. Arras,Patric Jensfelt,Yixi Cai,Iolanda Leite*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GESTO is a spatio-temporal memory that connects a persistent 4D scene graph with a two-level hierarchy of human–object interactions and events to support activity-grounded retrospective reasoning in human environments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current 4D scene graphs preserve object/place histories but lack activity structure; existing activity representations are either not grounded in persistent 3D scenes or rely on externally provided event boundaries and object associations. There is a need to fuse grounding, persistence, and causal activity structure for robust reasoning.

Method: Two-level hierarchy of atomic human–object interactions and goal-driven events. From an RGB-D stream, extract timestamped interactions, ground them to persistent scene entities, group them into events, and use event context to refine uncertain object associations. A relation-aware tool-calling agent queries the memory for activity-centric spatio-temporal reasoning.

Result: Evaluated on standard categories of an existing benchmark plus 40 new Space2Event and Event2Space queries. Scores ~0.71–0.75 on standard categories, approaching a ground-truth-grounded method; outperforming ablated variants lacking grounding or event inputs. Space2Event and Event2Space queries scored ~0.73–0.75. Ablations show hierarchical structure and context grounding refinement provide complementary benefits.

Conclusion: Activity-grounded hierarchical memory enables retrospective reasoning in dynamic human environments by grounding events in a persistent 4D scene and refining object associations through context.

Abstract: Robots operating in human environments need memories that capture not only what objects exist and where, but also how people use them over time and how individual interactions compose into goal-directed activities. Existing 4D scene graphs preserve object and place histories but omit activity structure, whereas activity representations are either not grounded in persistent 3D scenes or rely on externally provided event boundaries and object associations. We present GESTO (Grounded Event and Spatio-Temporal memOry), a spatio-temporal memory that couples a persistent 4D scene graph with a two-level hierarchy of atomic human--object interactions and goal-driven events. From an RGB-D observation stream, GESTO automatically extracts timestamped interactions, grounds them to persistent scene entities, groups them into events, and uses event context to refine uncertain object associations. A relation-aware tool-calling agent queries the resulting memory for activity-centric spatio-temporal reasoning. We evaluate GESTO on the reproducible text, binary, and time categories of an existing benchmark, together with 40 new Space2Event and Event2Space queries. GESTO achieves scores of 0.71, 0.75, and 0.70 on the standard categories, approaching a method supplied with ground-truth event and object grounding, while substantially outperforming the same reasoning framework when these inputs are removed. It further achieves 0.73 and 0.75 on Space2Event and Event2Space queries. Ablations show that hierarchical event structure and context-aware grounding refinement provide complementary benefits, supporting activity-grounded hierarchical memory for retrospective reasoning in dynamic human environments.

</details>


### [34] [SapiensID 2.0: Aligning Human Recognition Foundation Models with Human Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10497)
*Yiyang Su,Jie Zhu,Feng Liu,Anil K. Jain,Xiaoming Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SapiensID 2.0: a human recognition framework that fuses semantic and temporal cues via ITA, TND, and K-SAH, leveraging zero-shot MLLM knowledge to achieve state-of-the-art on image/video re-identification, gait, and robust face recognition without large-scale video data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Foundation models rely on static geometric features, causing semantic blindness, overfitting to transient noise, and underutilization of invariant soft biometrics; temporal motion signatures are under-exploited.

Method: Transfer zero-shot semantic knowledge from Multimodal Large Language Models into a discriminative embedding space; address dimensional mismatch with Invariant Trait Alignment (ITA); disentangle transient noise with Transient Noise Disentanglement (TND); introduce Kinematic Semantic Attention Head (K-SAH) to extend spatial attention over temporal windows and track semantic patches across time without requiring large video datasets.

Result: Empirical evaluation shows SapiensID 2.0 achieves state-of-the-art performance on image- and video-based person re-identification and gait recognition, while maintaining robust face recognition capabilities.

Conclusion: By integrating semantic and temporal representations through ITA, TND, and K-SAH and leveraging zero-shot MLLM knowledge, SapiensID 2.0 offers robust, scalable cross-modal human recognition with reduced dependence on extensive video data.

Abstract: While foundation models have significantly advanced human recognition across diverse modalities, they predominantly rely on static, geometric feature extraction. This approach fundamentally diverges from human perception. Consequently, current models often suffer from "semantic blindness," overfitting to transient noise while failing to leverage invariant soft biometrics, and struggle to capture temporal motion signatures. To bridge this gap, we propose SapiensID 2.0, a human recognition framework enriched with both semantic and temporal awareness. To overcome the lack of soft-biometric annotations, we transfer zero-shot semantic knowledge from Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) into a discriminative embedding space. We resolve the dimensional mismatch between these spaces using Invariant Trait Alignment (ITA) to distill core persistent traits, and Transient Noise Disentanglement (TND) to decouple artifacts like clothing. Furthermore, we design a Kinematic Semantic Attention Head (K-SAH) that extends spatial attention across temporal windows. By tracking semantic patches over time, K-SAH captures rich kinematic signatures without requiring large-scale video datasets. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SapiensID 2.0 achieves state-of-the-art performance across image- and video-based person re-identification and gait recognition, while maintaining robust face recognition capabilities.

</details>


### [35] [DSAR: Dual-Stream Autoregressive Modeling of Temporal Cloth Dynamics for Photorealistic Animatable Avatars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10500)
*Haozhong Xiong,Yao Yu,Yu Zhou,Sidan Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Dual-stream autoregressive model for animatable avatars that separates geometry and internal state with a memory bank, enabling temporally coherent cloth dynamics and better generalization to novel motions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current methods often learn pose-appearance correlations rather than true motion evolution, causing over-smoothed cloth and artifacts on unseen poses due to neglect of temporal causality in cloth physics.

Method: A dual-stream autoregressive framework: a geometric stream propagates surface displacement from previous frames; a state stream retrieves historical internal states from a memory bank and fuses them with current features; includes motion-adaptive aggregation and adaptive regularization for smoothness vs flexibility.

Result: Significant improvements in rendering quality, temporal consistency, and generalization to motion patterns beyond training distributions on challenging datasets.

Conclusion: Explicitly modeling temporal causality through dual-stream temporal dynamics yields realistic cloth dynamics and better generalization than traditional pose-centered approaches.

Abstract: Creating photorealistic and temporally coherent animatable human avatars from RGB videos remains challenging. Current methods struggle to capture realistic cloth dynamics, producing over-smoothed appearance or severe artifacts on out-of-distribution poses. This limitation stems from a fundamental oversight: existing approaches neglect the temporal causality inherent in cloth physics, where current states emerge from previous states through temporal evolution rather than instantaneous skeletal configurations alone. Without explicit modeling of this causal structure, networks learn pose-appearance correlations instead of motion evolution, leading to poor generalization. We introduce a dual-stream autoregressive framework that explicitly models both observable geometric information and implicit internal state. The geometric stream propagates surface displacement from the previous frame, while the state stream fuses current features with historical states retrieved from a memory bank. Motion-adaptive aggregation handles spatially-varying dynamics, and adaptive regularization balances smoothness with flexibility. Experiments on challenging datasets demonstrate significant improvements in rendering quality, temporal consistency, and generalization to motion patterns beyond training distributions, validating that dual-stream temporal modeling enables realistic cloth dynamics.

</details>


### [36] [Towards Color-Faithful Low-Light Image Enhancement via Adaptive Color Debiasing and Saturation Rectification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10512)
*Zhichen Yang,Rui Xu,Yuzhen Niu,Fusheng Li,Hui Da,Ri Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel cylindrical color correction framework (CAGE) for color-faithful low-light image enhancement, featuring AdaLAB (cylindrical LAB space) and AdaCCT (adaptive cylindrical color transform) with forward and inverse passes for debiasing and saturation rectification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Low-light imaging causes color bias and abnormal saturation due to low SNR and the image formation process; faithful color restoration is a key challenge alongside brightness enhancement.

Method: Propose CAGE with AdaLAB and AdaCCT. AdaLAB provides a decoupled, image-specific cylindrical LAB basis for uniform color correction. AdaCCT enables forward RGB->AdaLAB transform to suppress bias via chromatic-plane shifting/scaling and inverse transform to rectify saturation via out-of-gamut lightness compensation; the whole pipeline integrates with various backbones.

Result: Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks show improved color fidelity, reduced color bias and saturation abnormalities, and better perceptual quality across different low-light enhancement backbones; code released at given URL.

Conclusion: CAGE provides a robust, adaptive color correction framework for low-light image enhancement, delivering faithful colors while maintaining or improving brightness with compatibility to existing backbones.

Abstract: Low-light imaging often introduces color bias caused by the low signal-to-noise ratio and the image formation process. Although recent low-light image enhancement methods have achieved strong brightness recovery, faithful color restoration remains challenging, manifesting as overall color bias together with local under- and over-saturation. To address this issue, we propose CAGE, a cylindrical color correction framework with adaptive color debiasing and gamut-harmonized saturation rectification for color-faithful low-light image enhancement. We first introduce AdaLAB, a cylindrical adaptive LAB color space that provides a decoupled and image-specific basis for uniform color correction. Building on this color space, we further develop AdaCCT, an adaptive cylindrical color transform with forward and inverse transforms for the conversion between RGB and AdaLAB color space, as well as necessary color debiasing and saturation rectification. The forward transform suppresses embedded color bias before backbone enhancement by reorganizing the chromatic distribution through chromatic-plane shifting and scaling, while the inverse transform achieves faithful saturation rectification through out-of-gamut lightness compensation. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks show that CAGE achieves more faithful color restoration, specifically reduces color bias and saturation abnormality, and delivers better overall visual quality across different low-light enhancement backbones. The code is available at https://yangzhichen763.github.io/CAGE/.

</details>


### [37] [SafeCap: Improving LVLM Safety with Image Captioning Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10513)
*Caoyuan Ma,Wenpu Liu,Weichu Xie,Tian Gu,Shilei Zhao,Lingxi Min,Shuai Dong,Yuqi Xu,Ji Zhao,Ziyue Wang,Wenzheng Chang,Taiqiang Wu,Yongfu Zhu,Wenqi Shao,Yinqiang Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SafeCap uses a caption-first reinforcement-learning policy to align LVLMs for safer responses to visual prompts, improving safety while preserving vision utility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LVLMs remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks via visual inputs and inherit safety alignment from language backbones; a direct refusal strategy is limited. A caption-mediated approach can reveal and leverage visual cues that guide safe decision-making.

Method: Train a policy to produce a safety-relevant caption before generating the final answer. The caption is optimized by whether it enables a frozen LLM to reach a safety-aligned decision, effectively using a caption-mediated objective to expose visual cues relevant to safe responses under the DirectCap protocol. Compare against safety SFT, DPO, and SafeGRPO on matched backbones/datasets.

Result: SafeCap yields substantial safety improvements across five multimodal safety benchmarks and six vision-utility benchmarks, with 3.7–19.0 point gains in safety averages across four model settings, while maintaining or improving vision utility. In controlled back-to-back comparisons, it outperforms safety SFT, DPO, and SafeGRPO.

Conclusion: Caption-mediated reinforcement learning is effective for multimodal safety alignment in LVLMs, enabling safer responses by exposing safety-relevant visual cues through an intermediate caption before final answers, and outperforming existing safety fine-tuning methods under matched conditions.

Abstract: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks that exploit visual inputs to bypass safety alignment inherited from their language backbones. We propose SafeCap, a reinforcement-learning framework that aligns LVLMs through learned self-captioning. SafeCap trains a policy model to first generate a safety-relevant image caption and then produce a final answer; the caption is further optimized by whether it enables a frozen LLM to reach a safety-aligned decision. This caption-mediated objective encourages the policy to expose visual cues relevant to safe response generation rather than relying solely on direct refusal supervision. Across five multimodal safety benchmarks and six vision-utility benchmarks, SafeCap substantially improves aggregate safety performance under its intended DirectCap protocol, with gains of 3.7-19.0 points in safety average across four model settings while maintaining comparable or improved vision utility. Under controlled comparisons on matched backbones and data, SafeCap outperforms safety SFT, DPO, and SafeGRPO, demonstrating the effectiveness of caption-mediated reinforcement learning for multimodal safety alignment.

</details>


### [38] [SparSTAR: Sparse Attention for SpaceTime AutoRegressive Video Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10519)
*Jongbeom Lee,Hyunwoo Yu,Jincheol Yang,Jaemin Choi,Suk-Ju Kang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SparSTAR introduces a training-free block-sparse attention mechanism for InfinityStar's cross-scale video generation, enabling efficient attention across expensive scales while preserving generation fidelity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: InfinityStar's evolving scale and cross-clip context make late-scale attention costly and unreliable when reusing diffusion/image-variant patterns; there is a need for an efficient, reliable attention mechanism at expensive scales.

Method: SparSTAR scores contiguous key blocks from the current query and key activations for each expensive scale and attention head, retains required conditioning context, and processes the selected blocks through a forward-only sparse path. It recomputes block selection at each target scale rather than reusing a transferred mask, and analyzes cross-scale consistency, pattern persistence across clip boundaries, and fidelity degradation with increasing reuse distance.

Result: On 720p text-to-video and image-to-video generation, SparSTAR preserves every token and refinement scale, achieves about 1.6x end-to-end speedup, and maintains VBench and paired-output reconstruction fidelity close to dense InfinityStar.

Conclusion: A training-free block-sparse attention approach is effective for cross-scale video generation with InfinityStar; recomputing block masks per target scale yields more reliable attention than transferring masks, delivering notable speedups with minimal fidelity loss.

Abstract: InfinityStar extends visual autoregressive generation to video through a sequence of image and clip pyramids. Its changing scale and cross-clip context, however, leave late-scale attention costly and make sparse patterns reused from diffusion or image VAR models unreliable. We introduce SparSTAR, a training-free block-sparse attention method tailored to this setting. At each expensive scale and attention head, SparSTAR scores contiguous key blocks from the current query and key activations, retains required conditioning context, and executes the selected blocks through a forward-only sparse path. We analyze cross-scale consistency within a clip, pattern persistence across clip boundaries, and quality degradation as reuse spans increasingly distant scales. Across these analyses, important key blocks shift, showing that recomputing block selection at each target scale is more reliable than reusing a transferred mask. On 720p text-to-video and image-to-video generation, SparSTAR preserves every token and refinement scale while providing about a 1.6x end-to-end speedup and maintaining VBench and paired-output reconstruction fidelity close to dense InfinityStar.

</details>


### [39] [Unlocking the Power of Medical Tabular Data via Semantic-Aware Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10522)
*Yingsheng Liu,Haiming Li,Jingmin Zhu,Jiajun Sun,Victoria Mar,Monika Janda,H. Peter Soyer,Zongyuan Ge,Zhen Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A semantic-aware multimodal pretraining framework for medical text-image data that leverages structured tabular information to improve representation learning, achieving SOTA on dermatology and ophthalmology tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Structured clinical data in tabular form contains dense, quantitative diagnostic phenotypes that are underutilized by current semantic-agnostic, regression-based multimodal methods, hindering cross-domain generalization.

Method: Two components: (1) Importance-Aware Adaptive Masking to create a label-free curriculum by prioritizing salient features according to inter-feature diagnostic importance; (2) Soft-Label Discretized Module to replace unstable numerical regression with distribution matching, preserving ordinal relationships and addressing intra-feature continuity-discreteness.

Result: Extensive experiments on large-scale dermatology datasets (SLICE-3D, HOP) and ophthalmology (EyePACS) establish new state-of-the-art performance, with strong robustness and cross-domain generalization.

Conclusion: Modeling the intrinsic 2D structure of tabular data in a semantic-aware framework yields superior multimodal representations and generalization across medical domains.

Abstract: While vision-language models dominate medical representation learning, unstructured text lacks the dense, quantitative diagnostic phenotypes inherent in structured clinical tables. However, existing multimodal pre-training methods underutilize this potential due to semantic-agnostic designs that treat tabular inputs as flat vectors and employ unstable continuous regression objectives. To overcome this, we propose a novel semantic-aware framework explicitly modeling the intrinsic two-dimensional structure of tabular data. First, addressing the inter-feature hierarchy of varying diagnostic importance, we introduce Importance-Aware Adaptive Masking to construct a label-free curriculum prioritizing salient features. Second, addressing the intra-feature continuity-discreteness duality, we propose a Soft-Label Discretized Module that replaces unstable numerical regression with stable distribution matching, thereby mathematically preserving ordinal relationships. Extensive experiments across large-scale dermatology (SLICE-3D, HOP) and ophthalmology (EyePACS) datasets establish a new state-of-the-art (SOTA), demonstrating exceptional robustness and cross-domain generalizability.

</details>


### [40] [Rethinking Text-Based Image Retrieval in Specific Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10524)
*Jingyang Tan,Sheng Yang,Yuanpeng Chen,Jian Wang,Nianjin Ye,Chen Xing,Lanpeng Jia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces DSMM-TBIR with SecMM-TBIR benchmark for domain-specific multi-match TBIR, and SAFT (SASS+ISD) to address semantic compression and false negatives in ITC fine-tuning; achieves +7.8 mAP@20 on SecMM-TBIR and better generalization; benchmark to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Single-match TBIR benchmarks misrepresent practical performance in domains like surveillance where a query can match multiple images; domain-specific TBIR suffers from semantic compression and false negatives in vanilla contrastive learning, necessitating targeted data, supervision, and distillation strategies.

Method: DSMM-TBIR data engine enables domain-specific, multi-match retrieval; SecMM-TBIR dataset comprises 50k surveillance images with 200 queries; SAFT framework combines Semantic-Aware Soft-Label Supervision (SASS) and Intra-modal Structural Distillation (ISD) to learn domain-sensitive representations for CLIP-like models.

Result: SAFT delivers an average mAP@20 gain of 7.8 points on SecMM-TBIR over standard image-text contrastive (ITC) fine-tuning and improves performance on general-domain tasks.

Conclusion: The proposed benchmark and SAFT framework establish a promising paradigm for domain-specific TBIR; the SecMM-TBIR benchmark will be released to spur further research.

Abstract: Driven by the rapid advancement of vision-language representation learning, Text-based Image Retrieval (TBIR) has made notable progress. However, existing benchmarks are predominantly constructed on an exclusive single-match assumption between query and images. While effective in general scenarios, this assumption fails to reflect practical system performance in specific domains (e.g., surveillance), where a single query often corresponds to multiple relevant candidate images. To address this limitation, we design a Domain-Specific Multi-Match Text-based Image Retrieval (DSMM-TBIR) data engine. Leveraging this engine, we construct Security Multi-Match TBIR (SecMM-TBIR), a benchmark comprising 50k surveillance images with 200 comprehensive queries. Furthermore, we observe that vanilla contrastive learning in specific domains suffers from severe false negatives, forcing the model to push apart semantically similar pairs and thus degrading retrieval performance. We propose the Semantic-Aware Fine-Tuning (SAFT) framework to address semantic compression in specific domains, which incorporates Semantic-Aware Soft-Label Supervision (SASS) and Intra-modal Structural Distillation (ISD) to establish a promising paradigm for domain-specific TBIR tasks. Experiments across diverse CLIP-like models demonstrate that SAFT yields an average mAP@20 gain of 7.8 points on SecMM-TBIR over standard image-text contrastive (ITC) fine-tuning, while also improving general-domain performance. The entire benchmark will be released to facilitate further research.

</details>


### [41] [Dynamic Context Adapters: Efficiently Infusing History into Vision-and-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10525)
*Yuhang Song,Bor-Jiun Lin,Jiaxu Liu,Te-Chuan Chiu,Anh Nguyen,Chun-Yi Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Dynamic Context Adapter (DCA) introduces fixed-size, dynamically compressed memory to pretrained VLMs to inject historical context without frame concatenation, reducing attention cost and memory while boosting long-horizon performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-Language Models lack temporal understanding for sequential tasks; incorporating history with full-frame concatenation is quadratic in attention and memory. Existing approaches either inflate computation or cause information loss through temporal compression.

Method: DCA uses a fixed-size, dynamically compressed memory module that preserves historical semantics, enabling memory for pretrained VLMs without altering input frame order; it bridges static VLMs with recurrent policies.

Result: Achieves >25% reduction in attention FLOPs and ~13% memory savings; improves performance on long-horizon tasks.

Conclusion: DCA provides an efficient mechanism to imbue pretrained VLMs with memory-like capabilities for sequential reasoning, maintaining efficiency while enhancing temporal understanding.

Abstract: Historical context integration presents a fundamental challenge for Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in sequential decision-making tasks. Current VLMs process visual inputs independently, which creates critical limitations for downstream applications that require temporal understanding. Direct incorporation of historical frames into Transformer inputs produces quadratic attention complexity and excessive memory consumption. Existing approaches suffer from significant drawbacks: computational inflation or substantial information loss through temporal compression. To address these challenges, we introduce Dynamic Context Adapter (DCA), a novel context injection approach for pretrained VLMs. Our method employs fixed-size, dynamically compressed memory to preserve historical semantics without frame concatenation. DCA bridges static VLMs and recurrent policies and enables memory capabilities in pretrained models while maintaining computational efficiency. DCA achieves over $25\%$ reduction in attention FLOPs and $13\%$ memory savings while improving performance on long-horizon tasks.

</details>


### [42] [Flow Straight to Reality: Perceptually Consistent Flow Matching for Efficient Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10544)
*Sangwoo Jo,Donggeun Ko,Jayeon Kang,Youngsang Kwak,Jaehwa Kwak,Sungjoon Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PCFlow unifies perceptually balanced image restoration by directly modeling a continuous latent transport from degraded inputs to clean targets. It uses latent consistency flow (LCF) with LCPL constraints, plus a conflict-free gradient projection, and a lightweight convolutional backbone to achieve competitive results with lower compute.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the distortion–perception tradeoff in image restoration while avoiding expensive posterior sampling and multi-stage pipelines that are computationally heavy and architecturally complex.

Method: Direct parameterization of a continuous transport from degraded observations to clean targets via a latent consistency flow. Introduces Latent Consistency Perceptual Loss (LCPL) to constrain the guiding velocity toward sharp data manifolds. Employs a conflict-free gradient projection to stabilize multi-objective optimization. Uses a lightweight, convolution-only backbone.

Result: Demonstrates competitive performance across diverse restoration tasks at a fraction of traditional computational costs, with stable, few-step inference.

Conclusion: PCFlow delivers a unified, efficient framework that balances distortion and perceptual quality through a flow-based transport mechanism, perceptual constraints, and gradient projection, enabling practical, low-cost restoration.

Abstract: Image restoration is fundamentally constrained by the tradeoff between distortion and perception: minimizing pixel-wise error yields over-smoothed results, whereas optimizing for perceptual realism often introduces structural deviations. Recent approaches attempt to balance this tradeoff via posterior sampling or multi-stage generative pipelines, yet remain computationally expensive and architecturally complex. To overcome these limitations, we propose PCFlow (Perceptually Consistent Flow Matching), a unified framework that directly parameterizes a continuous transport from degraded observations to clean targets, jointly optimizing distortion and perceptual quality. While its latent consistency flow objective drives stable and efficient few-step inference, a Latent Consistency Perceptual Loss (LCPL) imposes semantic constraints directly on the guiding velocity field, steering the dynamics toward visually sharp data manifolds. Furthermore, recognizing the inherent conflict between structural and perceptual consistencies, we integrate a conflict-free gradient projection strategy to stabilize the multi-objective optimization landscape. Combined with lightweight, convolution-only backbone, PCFlow achieves competitive performance across diverse restoration tasks at a fraction of traditional computational costs.

</details>


### [43] [A HamNoSys-Guided Dataset and Baselines for Fine-Grained Isolated Handshape Recognition in Sign Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10588)
*Ushnish Sarkar,Suvajit Patra,Bhaswar Chattopadhyay,Pranab Singha Roy,Tapas Samanta*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Large HamNoSys-based handshape benchmark with 144k RGB images across 160 classes from 15 signers; evaluates appearance-based (ResNet-18, ViT-B/16) and landmark-based (GNN, XGBoost) models; LOSO reveals strong cross-signer generalization gap; external datasets tested for context; resource aims for reproducible fine-grained isolated-handshape research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address limitations of phonetically defined visual inventories and signer-aware evaluation in fine-grained handshape recognition; establish a language-independent, reproducible benchmark based on HamNoSys to advance sign-language technology.

Method: Collect and curate a balanced dataset of 144,000 RGB images from 15 participants for 160 HamNoSys handshape classes; evaluate appearance-based models (ResNet-18, ViT-B/16) and landmark-based models (Graph ConvNet, XGBoost) on both class-stratified subject-dependent and 15-fold LOSO splits; compare with LSWH100 and ASL Fingerspelling Dataset A for external context.

Result: Subject-dependent splits yield reproducible reference performance across all model families; LOSO evaluation reveals substantial generalization drop to unseen participants. On ASL Fingerspelling Dataset A, LOSO mean top-1 accuracy ranges from 82.20% to 87.40%.

Conclusion: Provides a reproducible benchmark resource (acquisition, curation, and evaluation protocols) for fine-grained isolated-handshape research and for developing more accessible sign-language technologies.

Abstract: Purpose: Fine-grained handshape recognition supports computational sign-language transcription, recognition, and translation, but broad, phonetically defined visual inventories with signer-aware evaluation remain limited. This work introduces a benchmark grounded in the language-independent Hamburg Notation System (HamNoSys). Methods: A balanced dataset of 144,000 RGB images was collected from 15 participants for 160 handshape classes defined by the official HamNoSys 4 Handshapes Chart. ResNet-18 and ViT-B/16 were evaluated as appearance-based models, while a graph convolutional network and XGBoost were evaluated from hand landmarks. Both a class-stratified subject-dependent split and a 15-fold leave-one-subject-out (LOSO) protocol were used. The same model families were additionally assessed on LSWH100 and ASL Fingerspelling Dataset A for external context. Results: The subject-dependent benchmarks established reproducible reference performance across all four model families, whereas LOSO evaluation exposed a substantial reduction when recognition was required to generalise to unseen participants. On ASL Fingerspelling Dataset A, mean LOSO top-1 accuracy ranged from 82.20% to 87.40%. Conclusion: The documented acquisition, curation, and complementary evaluation protocols pro-vide a reproducible resource for fine-grained isolated-handshape research and for developing more accessible sign-language technologies.

</details>


### [44] [$π$-SUB: A Physics-Informed Synthetic Underwater Benchmark Dataset for Underwater Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10589)
*Namritha Lasyapriya Maddali,Rajini Makam,Suresh Sundaram,Narasimhan Sundararajan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: π-SUB is a physics-informed framework that generates hyper-realistic, paired synthetic underwater benchmark datasets to bridge the synthetic-to-real gap for Underwater Image Enhancement (UIE); it spans Jerlov water types with depth-dependent components, demonstrates strong hyper-realism and generalizability across UIE architectures, and provides public code/dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To close the synthetic-to-real gap in UIE by providing a hyper-realistic, generalizable benchmark that covers diverse depth and water-type conditions.

Method: Extend the classical underwater image formation model with depth-dependent downwelling irradiance, biologically resolved absorption, and environmental scattering across all Jerlov water types, plus controllable residual phenomena. Generate the π-SUB dataset of paired synthetic underwater-reference images across shallow-to-deep and coastal-to-oceanic environments. Evaluate via hyper-realism (Fréchet Inception Distance) and generalizability using four UIE architectures trained on six datasets and tested on six real benchmarks.

Result: π-SUB achieves a global FID 46% lower than Syrea. Across four UIE architectures trained on six datasets and tested on six real benchmarks, π-SUB yields UIQM improvements of 4.18% over PHISWID (next best) and 9.46% over Syrea, and NIQE reductions of 48.78% and 23.98%, respectively.

Conclusion: π-SUB is a hyper-realistic and generalizable benchmark for advancing underwater image enhancement methods; code and dataset are publicly available.

Abstract: This paper presents $π$-SUB, a physics-informed framework for generating synthetic underwater benchmark datasets that bridges the synthetic-to-real gap for Underwater Image Enhancement (UIE). The proposed framework extends the classical underwater image formation model by incorporating depth-dependent downwelling irradiance, biologically resolved absorption, and environmental scattering across all ten Jerlov water types, together with independently controllable residual phenomena. Using this framework, the $π$-SUB dataset consists of paired synthetic underwater-reference images spanning shallow-to-deep and coastal-to-oceanic environments. Extensive simulation studies have been carried out to evaluate $π$-SUB along two criteria namely hyper-realism and generalizability. For hyper-realism, $π$-SUB attains a global Frechet Inception Distance (FID) that is 46% lower than Syrea. For generalizability, four state-of-the-art UIE architectures (FUnIE-GAN, Pix2Pix, PUIE-Net, and Phaseformer) are used for comparative evaluation of $π$-SUB. These models were independently trained on six datasets including one real and five synthetic datasets and tested on six real-world benchmarks datasets. Across four UIE architectures and six real benchmark datasets, $π$-SUB improves UIQM by 4.18% over PHISWID (next best) and 9.46% over Syrea (next best), while reducing NIQE by 48.78% and 23.98%, respectively. These results establish $π$-SUB as a hyper-realistic and generalizable benchmark for developing the next generation of underwater image enhancement methods. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/airl-iisc/pi-SUB

</details>


### [45] [Rethinking Data Efficiency in Industrial Dense Prediction: Pretraining Coherence, Not Inductive Bias, Determines ViTs Low-Data Advantage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10590)
*Haoran Sui,Yaoyuan Jia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Data-efficiency gap for Vision Transformers in industrial dense prediction is driven by pretraining incoherence between ImageNet-pretrained ViT backbones and COCO-pretrained CNN necks, not intrinsic self-attention deficits. An AlignBlock family for pyramid-level feature recalibration partially closes the gap. In domain-proximal scenes (>=200 samples), Swin-Graft can exceed CNN baselines (e.g., 703-shot mAP@50: 0.973 vs 0.956 for YOLOv11x); in domain-distant scenes CNNs still hold advantage (141-shot: 0.900 vs 0.600). Grafted necks can yield up to 2.5x the mAP of random neck initialization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Question the assumption that Vision Transformers (ViTs) inherently require more labeled data for industrial dense prediction; identify the source of any data-efficiency gap and propose methods to align cross-architecture features to improve ViT-based detectors on domain-specific tasks.

Method: Controlled experiments on four industrial datasets comparing ViT-based detectors with CNN-based baselines. Analysis of pretraining incoherence between ImageNet-pretrained ViT backbones and COCO-pretrained CNN necks. Development of AlignBlock, a lightweight pyramid-level feature recalibration module, and evaluation of grafted neck weights versus random (untrained) necks. Comparison against strong CNN baselines (e.g., YOLOv11x) across varying shot counts and domain distances.

Result: Identification of a data-efficiency frontier: domain-proximal scenes with ≥200 samples favor ViT-based detectors when necks are aligned (Swin-Graft achieves 0.973 mAP@50 at 703 shots vs 0.956 for YOLOv11x); domain-distant scenes favor CNNs (0.900 vs 0.600 at 141 shots). Grafted neck weights provide up to 2.5x higher mAP than randomly initialized necks.

Conclusion: The data-efficiency gap for industrial dense prediction arises primarily from pretraining incoherence between backbones and necks rather than self-attention limits. AlignBlock-based recalibration and grafted necks can substantially improve ViT-based detectors in domain-proximal settings; performance advantage diminishes in domain-distant scenarios where CNNs remain competitive.

Abstract: Vision Transformers (ViTs) are widely believed to require more labeled data than CNNs for industrial dense prediction. Through controlled experiments on four industrial datasets, we show that the data-efficiency gap stems from pretraining incoherence, which refers to the statistical mismatch between ImageNet-pretrained ViT backbones and COCO-pretrained CNN necks, rather than from inherent self-attention deficits. We characterize the cross-architecture feature gap and propose a lightweight AlignBlock family for pyramid-level feature recalibration. Our core finding empirically identifies a data-efficiency frontier: for domain-proximal scenes with >= 200 samples, Swin-Graft surpasses YOLOv11x (terminal 703-shot: 0.973 vs 0.956 mAP@50); for domain-distant scenes, CNNs retain advantage (hook 141-shot: 0.900 vs 0.600 mAP@50). Grafted neck weights yield up to 2.5x the mAP of a randomly initialized neck.

</details>


### [46] [Gaussian Sculpting: End-to-End Controllable Surface Reconstruction via Field Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10602)
*Ke Jiaxin,Juncheng Liu,Yi Wang,Zhouhui Lian,Bin Liu,Shengfa Wang,Xiangjia He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Gaussian Sculpting introduces a differentiable, bi-level optimization framework for high-fidelity surface reconstruction in 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) by anchoring Gaussians to an evolving SDF surface. It uses an outer loop for geometry (SDF) optimization and an inner loop for Gaussian updates, imposes Gaussian-parameter constraints, and employs octree-like multi-resolution subdivision to preserve details and reduce memory. The method removes redundant surfaces, recovers structures from limited viewpoints, and achieves strong quality at low resolutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address surface reconstruction deficiencies in 3D Gaussian Splatting: geometric errors from viewpoint limitations and irregular Gaussian primitives that are hard to correct post hoc; enable end-to-end differentiable optimization that jointly refines geometry and primitives.

Method: Propose Gaussian Sculpting: anchor Gaussian primitives onto an evolving differentiable surface (SDF) so Gaussians guide SDF optimization rather than surface extraction; bi-level training with outer loop optimizing the SDF geometry and inner loop updating Gaussians with fixed geometry; constrain Gaussian parameters to stay consistent with the surface; introduce a multi-resolution octree-like subdivision to balance detail preservation with memory efficiency.

Result: Demonstrated on object-level scenes: removal of redundant surfaces, recovery of structures missing due to limited viewpoints, and strong reconstruction quality even at relatively low resolutions.

Conclusion: Gaussian Sculpting provides a stable, end-to-end differentiable framework that improves both geometric and appearance fidelity in 3DGS reconstructions, achieves effective surface recovery under limited viewpoints, and benefits from memory-efficient multi-resolution subdivision.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has recently enabled real-time novel view synthesis with impressive quality. However, it struggles to recover accurate surfaces under limited viewpoints and due to the inherent irregularity of Gaussian primitives. The resulting geometric errors are notoriously difficult to correct manually. To address these issues, we propose Gaussian Sculpting, a fully differentiable end-to-end framework for high-quality surface reconstruction. Our key insight is to anchor Gaussians onto an evolving differentiable surface, allowing them to guide signed distance field (SDF) optimization instead of extracting the surface only during post-processing. To enable stable gradient isolation during joint optimization, we design a bi-level training strategy in which the outer loop optimizes the geometry represented by the SDF, while the inner loop updates the Gaussians with the geometry fixed. We further impose constraints on Gaussian parameters to ensure consistency with the underlying surface, thereby improving both geometric and appearance fidelity during optimization. In addition, we introduce a multi-resolution subdivision scheme based on octree-like partitioning to preserve fine details while reducing memory consumption. Experiments on object-level scenes demonstrate that our method effectively removes redundant surfaces, recovers missing structures caused by limited viewpoints, and achieves strong reconstruction quality even at relatively low resolutions.

</details>


### [47] [InSight-doc: Agentic Visual Perception for Long-Document Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10628)
*Kaican Li,Weiyan Xie,Lewei Yao,Jiannan Wu,Lanqing Hong,Yongxiang Huang,Nevin L. Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: InSight-doc is an adaptive visual-perception framework for long-document understanding that starts at low resolution and selectively zooms into informative high-resolution regions, trained with SFT and RL on an active-perception corpus, achieving substantial accuracy gains, reduced hallucination, and lower latency; code and models are released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long, visually rich documents require reasoning over many pages, which is costly and prone to context rot and hallucination. A dynamic visual-resolution strategy can allocate perceptual resources efficiently without external retrievers.

Method: Propose InSight-doc, an agentic framework that starts from low visual resolution and selectively zooms into high-resolution regions for evidence. No external retriever. Train with an active-perception corpus: 17.9K SFT examples with region-level zoom trajectories and 19.2K hard RL examples. Use SFT+RL to obtain InSight-doc-8B.

Result: On document VQA benchmarks, achieve 4.3–16.4 accuracy point gains over the baseline. On long documents, hallucinations reduced by >40% and inference latency reduced by 41%–68%, while maintaining an accuracy lead.

Conclusion: Adaptive visual-perception via selective zooming is effective for long-document understanding; results suggest improved reliability and efficiency. Code, datasets, and model are publicly released.

Abstract: Long-document understanding often requires reasoning over many visually rich pages, making inference costly and prone to context rot. In this work, we propose InSight-doc, an agentic visual perception framework that treats visual resolution as an adaptive reasoning-time resource. InSight-doc starts from low resolution and selectively zooms into high-resolution regions for finer evidence, without relying on any external retriever. To train such an agent, we construct an active-perception corpus of 17.9K high-quality SFT examples with region-level zoom-in trajectories, accompanied by 19.2K hard RL examples. Through SFT+RL, InSight-doc-8B improves the baseline by 4.3--16.4 accuracy points over document VQA benchmarks. On long documents, it reduces hallucination by more than 40% and inference latency by 41%--68% while maintaining an accuracy lead. Our code, datasets, and model are released at https://github.com/m-Just/InSight-doc .

</details>


### [48] [MedUP: Awakening Unified Understanding and Perception in Medical Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10635)
*Yuan Wang,Hualiang Wang,Yixin Chen,Songtao Jiang,Shujian Gao,Jiaming Lin,Siming Fu,Jian Wu,Zuozhu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A unified Med-VLM (MedUP) integrates perception and language understanding via a region tokenizer (UniMedTok) that encodes masks as discrete tokens within the LLM vocabulary. It is trained on UniMed-Train (1.84M instances) for text-guided segmentation, grounding, VQA, and CoT-based segmentation, and evaluated with UniMed-Bench. Empirically, MedUP surpasses native, agentic, and dual-decoder Med-VLMs across tasks and competes with specialist segmentors, highlighting the viability of unified perception-understanding models in medical vision-language tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Med-VLMs face a gap between perception and language understanding: some merely verbalize regions as coordinate strings, while others rely on external modules that decouple perception from reasoning, creating alignment gaps. A unified model in a shared token space could bridge perception and language.

Method: Introduce UniMedTok, a region tokenizer that encodes masks as discrete tokens in the LLM vocabulary, allowing seamless interleaving of mask tokens with text. Develop UniMed-Train (1.84M instances) covering text-guided segmentation, region-grounded understanding, medical VQA, and CoT-based segmentation. Propose UniMed-Bench for unified evaluation. Train MedUP to operate in a unified token space that unifies perception and understanding.

Result: MedUP outperforms native, agentic, and dual-decoder Med-VLMs on the evaluated tasks, and remains competitive with specialist segmentors.

Conclusion: Unified perception and understanding in medical VLMs is viable and advantageous. The region-tokenizer approach and unified dataset/benchmark enable stronger region-language alignment and task versatility, suggesting a promising direction for Med-VLM research.

Abstract: Medical Vision-Language Models (Med-VLMs) excel at verbalizing visual content, yet precise visual perception, segmentation, and grounding remain challenging. Existing approaches either verbalize regions as coordinate strings or rely on external modules that decouple perception from understanding, creating representation gaps for region-language alignment. We present MedUP, a Med-VLM that natively unifies perception and understanding within a shared token space. At its core lies UniMedTok, a region tokenizer that encodes masks as discrete tokens in the LLM vocabulary, enabling the model to seamlessly interleave mask tokens with text. We curate UniMed-Train, a 1.84M-instance corpus spanning text-guided segmentation, region-grounded understanding, medical VQA and CoT-based segmentation, and introduce UniMed-Bench for unified evaluation. Extensive experiments show that MedUP outperforms native, agentic, and dual-decoder Med-VLMs across all tasks while remaining competitive with specialist segmentors, demonstrating the strong potential of unified understanding and perception modeling.

</details>


### [49] [PolypVision: A Three-Stage Hierarchical Deep Learning Framework for Classification and Segmentation of Colorectal Polyps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10649)
*Hamidreza Bolhasani,Hamidreza Rastad,Amir Mohammad Akbari,Mohammad Tashakoripour,Parnian Asadollahi,Ata Khodami,Mojgan Forootan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PolypVision is a three-stage DL pipeline for colorectal polyp analysis (classification, segmentation, adenoma-subtype classification) using EfficientNetV2-M and UNet++; achieves high performance (AUC ~0.99; mAP@50 94.4%); device-independent and publicly available as a web app.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate, automated detection, segmentation, and histology-like classification of colorectal polyps across diverse endoscopic imaging without hardware-specific adaptation, by combining stage-specific tasks with transfer learning.

Method: Stage 1: binary polyp classification (adenomatous vs hyperplastic) plus Paris/JNet classification using EfficientNetV2-M with Focal Loss. Stage 2: segmentation with a UNet++ decoder using Stage 1 backbone as encoder; Dice and BCE losses. Stage 3: adenoma subtype classification (tubular, tubulovillous, villous) using EfficientNetV2-M with transfer learning from Stage 2. Evaluated on PolypGen, Kvasir-SEG, CVC-ClinicDB. Grad-CAM used for interpretability. Device-independent, web app deployment (DataBioX).

Result: AUC ~0.99 for frame classification; mAP@50 = 94.4% on Kvasir-SEG; outperforms or matches state-of-the-art methods; Grad-CAM shows clinically relevant attention; platform operates across diverse imaging systems; policy-free access via web app.

Conclusion: A hierarchical, transfer-learning-driven pipeline with task-specific losses yields robust, device-independent, clinically meaningful automated colorectal polyp analysis, with public web deployment.

Abstract: Colorectal cancer (CRC) remains one of the leading causes of cancer-related mortality worldwide, predominantly arising from precancerous polyps. Accurate detection, segmentation, and endoscopic and histological classification of colorectal polyps are crucial for timely clinical intervention. In this study, we present PolypVision, a three-stage hierarchical deep learning framework that sequentially performs: (Stage 1) binary classification of polyps as adenomatous or hyperplastic, with simultaneous Paris and JNet classification, using EfficientNetV2-M with Focal Loss; (Stage 2) polyp segmentation with recommended resection method using a UNet++ decoder with the Stage 1 backbone as encoder, optimized with Dice and BCE losses; and (Stage 3) adenoma subtype classification (tubular, tubulovillous, villous) using EfficientNetV2-M with transfer learning from Stage 2. Evaluated on three public datasets -- PolypGen, Kvasir-SEG, and CVC-ClinicDB -- PolypVision achieves an AUC of approximately 0.99 for frame classification and a detection mAP@50 of 94.4% on Kvasir-SEG, outperforming or matching state-of-the-art methods. Gradient-weighted Class Activation Maps (Grad-CAM) confirm that the model attends to clinically relevant lesion features. The framework is device-independent, operating across diverse endoscopic imaging systems without hardware-specific adaptation. These results demonstrate that a hierarchical, transfer-learning-driven pipeline with task-specific loss functions offers a robust, device-independent, and clinically meaningful approach to automated colorectal polyp analysis. PolypVision is freely available as a web application at https://polypvision.com, a DataBioX initiative, with a free usage tier open to all users.

</details>


### [50] [Cross-View Sequential Visual Localization with Spatio-Temporal Context Modeling for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10660)
*Jiaping Wang,Shaobo Li,Zhen Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Temporal-context-enhanced cross-view sequence localization uses a recurrent cross-frame module to aggregate historical context, boosting coarse ground features for satellite-region classification and enabling fine-grained local offset estimation. It yields substantial accuracy gains on CVIS, KITTI-CVL transfer, and zero-shot real-world tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit temporal information in cross-view localization, addressing degradation due to dynamic occlusion, illumination variation, and repetitive textures that limit frame-by-frame methods.

Method: A recurrent cross-frame module aggregates history to enhance current-frame coarse ground features, guiding satellite candidate-region classification; hierarchical fine-grained features enable precise local offset estimation; trained end-to-end for cross-view localization under temporal context.

Result: CVIS: mean localization error reduced from 3.80 m to 1.57 m; R@1 m from 8.14% to 40.22%. KITTI-CVL direct transfer yields mean error 2.61 m, further reduced to 2.27 m with target-domain fine-tuning. Zero-shot field tests achieve mean error 2.84 m and R@5 m 96.86%.

Conclusion: Temporal context enhancement significantly improves cross-view localization accuracy and robustness, enabling reliable deployment on public benchmarks and real-world roads.

Abstract: Continuous and reliable localization is essential for autonomous driving. Cross-view visual localization matches ground images with satellite maps, providing complementary localization cues for pipelines that depend on Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) signals and high-definition (HD) maps. Most existing cross-view visual localization methods process each frame independently, leaving temporal information underused and limiting accuracy under dynamic occlusion, illumination variation, and repetitive textures. This study proposes a temporal-context-enhanced framework for cross-view sequence visual localization. The proposed recurrent cross-frame module aggregates historical context from the previous state to enhance the coarse ground feature of each current frame. These enhanced features facilitate satellite candidate-region classification, while hierarchical fine-grained features enable precise local offset estimation. On the CVIS dataset, the proposed method reduces mean localization error from 3.80 m to 1.57 m and increases R@1 m from 8.14% to 40.22%. Direct transfer to KITTI-CVL achieves a mean error of 2.61 m, with target-domain fine-tuning further reducing the mean error to 2.27 m. Zero-shot field experiments on a real-world vehicle achieve a mean error of 2.84 m and R@5 m of 96.86%. These results demonstrate that temporal context enhancement significantly improves cross-view localization accuracy and supports robust deployment on public benchmarks and real-world roads.

</details>


### [51] [Chartography: A Benchmark for Professional Chart Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10677)
*Suhaas Garre,Chris Mutty,Sushant Mehta,Edwin Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Chartography: a 100-task benchmark of domain-specific charts for professional decision-making, revealing current frontier models lag (mean pass@1 ≈ 45%) with widespread visual-perception errors; dataset and evaluation tools released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current chart benchmarks emphasize common chart types and short reasoning chains, failing to test professional-level chart-reading abilities and domain-specific chart conventions. There is a need for a challenging benchmark that assesses real-world chart understanding across diverse formats.

Method: Construct 100 tasks from professional practice, using domain-specific chart formats. Questions are authored by professionals who interpret these charts and independently verified by three experts. Evaluation across 30 frontier-model configurations, with 20 trials per task, assessing pass@1.

Result: Best frontier-model configuration achieves 45.0% mean pass@1; observed range 9.0–39.5%. Failures cluster in visual perception (subtle features, sparse axes labeling, 3D geometry projections, domain conventions).

Conclusion: Chartography provides a rigorous, open benchmark for professional-chart understanding and exposes substantial gaps in current models, highlighting the need for advances in visual perception and domain-conformant reasoning. All tasks, images, provenance, and evaluation code are released.

Abstract: Professionals across medicine, engineering, finance, manufacturing, and the sciences often make consequential decisions from charts. Existing chart benchmarks do not sufficiently measure this ability: they are dominated by bar, line, and pie formats, rely on shorter reasoning chains, and are nearing saturation, with frontier models already scoring 80-90%. We introduce Chartography, a benchmark of 100 tasks that pair charts drawn from professional practice, in domain-specific formats that standard chart benchmarks rarely include, with questions written by professionals who read these charts for a living and independently verified by three additional experts. In an evaluation of 30 frontier-model configurations (20 scored trials per task), the best configuration reaches only 45.0% mean pass@1; the remainder span 9.0-39.5%. Failures concentrate in visual perception: models can miss nuanced features, misread values along sparsely labeled axes, mishandle projected 3D geometry, and violate domain conventions encoded in the chart. We release all tasks, images, provenance metadata, and evaluation code.

</details>


### [52] [Bridging Severe Cross-Modal Misalignment: End-to-End Visible-Infrared Object Detection via Explicit Feature-Domain Affine Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10680)
*Qi Ming,Yuyang Wang,Mingjing Zhao,Yifan Xiao,Zhixin Guo,Zhiqiang Zhou,Peng Sun,Juan Fang,Fuqiang Yang,Xudong Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: JFRDet is an end-to-end visible-infrared detector that explicitly handles severe cross-modal misalignment via a Cross-Modal Affine Alignment (CMAA) module, illumination-aware fusion (IGCF), and an Alignment Quality-Consistency Gating (AQCG) mechanism, plus a new DVMA benchmark; achieves SOTA 69.7% mAP50 on DVMA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cross-modal spatial misalignment and illumination variability degrade RGB-IR object detection. Existing methods rely on implicit feature adaptation, which struggles with large offsets. A framework combining explicit alignment, reliable fusion, and stable joint optimization is proposed to robustly detect objects under severe cross-modal discrepancies.

Method: CMAA estimates an image-level affine transformation to align multi-level features across modalities. IGCF adaptively weighs RGB and thermal cues during fusion based on illumination conditions to improve cross-modal reliability. AQCG modulates detection supervision according to alignment reliability and gradient consistency to stabilize joint optimization. A new DVMA benchmark evaluates performance under severe misalignment.

Result: The approach reports 69.7% mAP50 on DVMA, claiming state-of-the-art performance, with code and DVMA dataset to be released on GitHub.

Conclusion: Explicit cross-modal affine alignment plus illumination-aware fusion and an alignment-quality gating strategy yield robust visible-infrared detection under large geometric misalignment, supported by a new challenging DVMA benchmark.

Abstract: Visible-infrared object detection relies on complementary RGB and thermal cues, but its performance is often degraded by cross-modal spatial misalignment.
