Most existing methods rely on implicit feature adaptation to handle weakly misaligned scenarios, while large-offset geometric discrepancies remain insufficiently addressed. In this paper, we propose a Joint Feature-domain Registration and Detection network (JFRDet), an end-to-end visible-infrared oriented object detector tailored for severely cross-modal geometric discrepancies. JFRDet introduces a Cross-Modal Affine Alignment (CMAA) module to estimate an image-level affine transformation for explicit multi-level feature alignment. Note that illumination changes directly affect the reliability of RGB cues, an Illumination-Guided Complementary Fusion (IGCF) module adaptively exploits modality reliability under varying illumination conditions for cross-modal fusion. Then, an Alignment Quality-Consistency Gating (AQCG) strategy stabilizes joint optimization by modulating detection supervision according to alignment reliability and gradient consistency. We further construct DroneVehicle Misaligned (DVMA), a benchmark for evaluating visible-infrared oriented object detection under severe cross-modal geometric misalignment. The proposed JFRDet achieves 69.7\% $\mathrm{mAP}_{50}$ on DVMA, which represents state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance. The code and dataset will be available on GitHub.

</details>


### [53] [Visual Geometry Foundation-Aware Gaussians for Single-Frame Surround-View Driving Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10682)
*Junhong Lin,Jinlong Wang,Xianda Guo,Yanlun Peng,Wei Zheng,Guoqing Liu,Hanli Wang,Tiesong Zhao,Wei Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VGGD is a geometry-prior–aware 3D Gaussian Splatting framework for single-frame surround-view reconstruction that shifts geometric modeling to the frontend by leveraging pretrained visual geometry priors, a dual-path neck to separate geometry from appearance, scale warmup for stable early geometry learning, and a hybrid pixel–volume decoder for novel-view rendering; it achieves superior rendering quality and better geometric consistency on nuScenes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Single-frame surround-view reconstruction suffers from severe geometric instability and artifacts due to minimal camera overlap. Existing methods rely on complex decoders or auxiliary cues and still suffer from weak geometric capacity of upstream features. Incorporating pretrained visual geometry priors can strengthen upstream representations and alleviate geometric ambiguity.

Method: Introduce VGGD. Use VGGT to supply transferable multi-view geometric prior tokens. Employ a Dual-Path Neck to decouple geometry-consistent and appearance-aware representations. Apply Scale Warmup to stabilize early geometry learning and reduce scale drift with ego-pose changes. Use a hybrid pixel–volume Gaussian decoder to render a 3D Gaussian scene for novel-view synthesis.

Result: On the nuScenes single-frame benchmark, VGGD achieves the best overall rendering quality among compared methods and demonstrates improved relative geometric consistency.

Conclusion: Shifting geometric modeling to the frontend with foundation priors improves stability and rendering in sparse surround views. VGGD demonstrates effective use of pretrained geometry priors, dual-path representation, and scale stabilization to produce high-quality, geometrically consistent novel-view reconstructions.

Abstract: Single-frame surround-view reconstruction faces severe geometric instability and rendering artifacts due to minimal inter-camera overlap. While existing methods rely on complex decoders or auxiliary cues, they remain bottlenecked by the weak geometric capacity of upstream features. We argue that leveraging pretrained visual geometry priors strengthens upstream representations and alleviates the geometric ambiguity in sparse surround views. To this end, we propose VGGD, a visual geometry foundation-aware 3D Gaussian Splatting framework for feed-forward surround-view driving reconstruction, which shifts geometric modeling to the frontend and adapts foundation priors to the driving camera setting. First, VGGD leverages VGGT to provide transferable multi-view geometric prior tokens. Next, we introduce a Dual-Path Neck to decouple geometry-consistent and appearance-aware representations, improving appearance completion in weakly observed regions. We further apply Scale Warmup to stabilize early geometry learning and suppress scale drift under ego-pose changes. Finally, we use a hybrid pixel--volume Gaussian decoder to produce a renderable 3D Gaussian scene for novel-view synthesis. Experiments on the nuScenes single-frame benchmark show that VGGD achieves the best overall rendering quality among the compared methods and improves relative geometric consistency.

</details>


### [54] [Embedding Rotation Invariance for Provable Multi-Oriented Scene Text Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10684)
*Zhibin Ma,Pengwen Dai,Yi Liu,Xugong Qin,Chenyun Yu,Xiaochun Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes a fully rotation-invariant scene text recognition network (RISTER) by embedding rotation equivariance in the encoder and rotation invariance in the decoder, with a rotation-invariant cross-attention-based decoder and a rotation-equivariant local-global encoder, achieving state-of-the-art results on standard and multi-oriented benchmarks without extra inference cost or orientation correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-oriented text in real scenes causes errors in STR; existing rotation-aware methods suffer from error accumulation, higher computation, and dependence on data; aim to provide theoretical rotation invariance guarantees to improve robustness and reduce reliance on orientation corrections.

Method: An encoder-decoder architecture where the encoder uses a rotation-equivariant local-global extraction network (combining equivariant convolutions and self-attention) and the decoder employs a rotation-invariant cross-attention mechanism to map features to text in a rotation-invariant way, yielding an end-to-end Rotation-Invariant Scene Text Recognition network (RISTER) with theoretical guarantees and no extra inference cost.

Result: RISTER achieves state-of-the-art performance on standard and multi-oriented benchmarks, surpassing the second-best model by 4.0 percentage points in accuracy on the general multi-oriented dataset.

Conclusion: The proposed rotation-invariant framework provides robustness to multi-oriented text with theoretical guarantees, reducing reliance on orientation correction while maintaining or improving performance, and showing strong empirical gains on both standard and multi-oriented STR benchmarks.

Abstract: Multi-oriented text is ubiquitous in real-world scenes and remains a major challenge for scene text recognition (STR). Existing rotation-aware methods explicitly estimate text orientation. However, due to the lack of theoretical guarantees, they are prone to error accumulation, increased computational cost, and strong reliance on data. In this work, we incorporate rotation invariance into the STR framework to address these limitations. Specifically, we adopt an encoder-decoder architecture, embedding rotation equivariance in the encoder and rotation invariance in the decoder to construct a fully rotation-invariant network. On the decoder side, we first identify and prove the rotation-invariant property of the cross-attention mechanism and use it to formulate a rotation-invariant text decoder that maps visual features to output text in a rotation-invariant manner. On the encoder side, we propose a rotation-equivariant local-global extraction network that integrates deep equivariant convolutions with self-attention, enabling rotation-equivariant feature extraction while modeling inter-character dependencies and preserving fine-grained visual details. By integrating the encoder and decoder, we obtain an end-to-end Rotation-Invariant Scene Text Recognition network (RISTER). RISTER provides rotation invariance with theoretical guarantees, enhancing robustness on multi-oriented samples without introducing additional inference computation or relying on data-driven orientation correction. Experiments show that RISTER achieves state-of-the-art performance on both standard and multi-oriented benchmarks, surpassing the second-best model by 4.0 percent in accuracy on the general multi-oriented dataset.

</details>


### [55] [MMArt A Multi-Perspective Multimodal Dataset for Visual Art Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10706)
*Shuai Wang,Wangyuan Ding,Yixian Shen,Jia-Hong Huang,Stevan Rudinac,Monika Kackovic,Nachoem Wijnberg,Marcel Worring*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MMArt is a large-scale dataset of 74,234 WikiArt paintings annotated from four independent perspectives (narrative, formal, historical, affective) plus a harmonized caption, revealing that different perspectives encode distinct information and that no single perspective suffices for art understanding; this motivates multi-perspective modeling and dataset design.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Artifact interpretation in vision-language art research is limited by single-perspective datasets; to enable richer analysis and generation, a multi-perspective dataset is needed that captures narrative, formal, historical, and emotional dimensions for the same artworks.

Method: Annotate each painting with four independent perspectives and a unified caption, using specialized vision-language models or human annotation, and validate via complementary quality evaluations. Conduct complementarity analyses to show information distinctness, generative analysis to link perspectives to reconstruction quality, discriminative retrieval analysis to measure task performance, and leave-one-out analysis to assess perspective replaceability.

Result: MMArt contains 74,234 paintings with four perspectives plus unified captions; perspectives show complementarity and distinct information. Formal analysis best preserves compositional style; historical descriptions yield strong affect in reconstructed images. In retrieval, narrative descriptions drive performance (R@1=44.0%), while formal descriptions are weak discriminators (R@1=7.8%). Leave-one-out shows historical descriptions are least replaceable across tasks.

Conclusion: A single perspective is insufficient for comprehensive art understanding; multi-perspective MMArt design is necessary. The dataset and code are publicly available to foster multi-perspective modeling and evaluation in art-language research.

Abstract: Recent vision-language models demonstrate impressive general visual understanding, yet their art interpretation remains shallow: they describe surface content but struggle with formal analysis, grounded historical interpretation, or affective characterization. We argue this is not only a model but also a dataset limitation. Existing art datasets are single perspective resources, where no dataset provides narrative, formal, emotional, and historical perspectives simultaneously for the same artworks. We introduce MMArt, a large-scale dataset of 74,234 WikiArt paintings, each annotated with four independently annotated perspectives plus a harmonized unified caption, produced by specialized vision-language models or human annotation and validated through complementary quality evaluations. Two complementarity analyses establish that perspectives encode genuinely distinct information. A generative analysis shows that formal analysis descriptions best preserve compositional style, and historical descriptions carry strong affective signal in reconstructed images. A discriminative retrieval analysis reveals task-asymmetry: narrative descriptions drive retrieval (R@1 = 44.0%), while formal descriptions, strongest for reconstruction, are nearly nondiscriminative at retrieval scale (R@1 = 7.8%). Leave-one-out analysis further confirms that historical descriptions are the least replaceable perspective across both tasks. Together, the two analyses establish that no single perspective suffices for all tasks, directly motivating MMArt multi-perspective design. The dataset, code, and additional information are available at https://shuaiwang97.github.io/MMArt/.

</details>


### [56] [Self-Geometry: GT-Free and Plug-and-Play Test-Time Adaptation for Geometrically Consistent 3D Vision Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10708)
*Seokhyun Youn,Dahyeon Kye,Sung-Ho Bae,Jihyong Oh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A plug-and-play test-time adaptation method (Self-Geometry) imposes explicit multi-view geometry constraints on vision foundation models via 2D pixel correspondences, improving pose and geometry across multiple VFMs and benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explicit multi-view geometric consistency is expensive to enforce during pretraining, and relying on implicit self-consistency at test time yields limited gains when the pretrained VFM is inaccurate. A lightweight, explicit TTA approach can improve geometry and pose without full bundle adjustment.

Method: Self-Geometry introduces: (1) Geometric Disentanglement Optimization that combines Multi-View Consistency and Epipolar Consistency losses with Gradient Disentanglement to avoid gradient conflicts; (2) Frame Angular-Neighbor, a view sampler based on SO(3) geodesic distances to lightly impose constraints; (3) Lightweight TTA, which adapts VFMs via LoRA. It uses 2D pixel correspondences as pseudo ground-truth.

Result: Consistent improvements in both pose and geometry estimation across six VFMs (VGGT, π^3, DA3-Giant/Large/Base/Small) and four benchmarks (7Scenes, ETH3D, ScanNet++, HiRoom).

Conclusion: Self-Geometry provides an efficient, plug-and-play TTA that enforces explicit multi-view geometric constraints, yielding robust gains across architectures and datasets without heavy optimization.

Abstract: Recent Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) predict depth, camera pose, and pointmap in a single forward pass without per-scene optimization, achieving strong generalization. However, enforcing explicit multi-view geometric consistency, e.g., through bundle adjustment, is computationally costly and is thus not imposed during VFM pretraining, so such inconsistency can arise. To address this, implicit self-consistency derived from model outputs (e.g., pointmaps, features), though enforced at test-time in prior work, delivers inherently limited performance gain, especially on scenes where the pretrained VFM is highly inaccurate. In contrast to this implicit signal, we propose Self-Geometry, a plug-and-play test-time adaptation pipeline that directly imposes explicit multi-view geometric constraints using 2D pixel correspondences as pseudo ground-truth. Our proposed Self-Geometry consists of Geometric Disentanglement Optimization, which combines Multi-View Consistency and Epipolar Consistency losses with Gradient Disentanglement to prevent gradient conflict; Frame Angular-Neighbor, a view sampler based on SO(3) geodesic distances for lightly imposing these constraints; and Lightweight TTA, which adapts VFMs via LoRA. Our method achieves consistent improvements in both pose and geometry estimation across six VFMs (VGGT, $π^3$, DA3-Giant/Large/Base/Small) and four benchmarks (7Scenes, ETH3D, ScanNet++, HiRoom).

</details>


### [57] [Compact Feed-Forward 3D Gaussians via Saliency-Guided Primitive Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10712)
*Tim-Felix Fassch,Jochen Kall,Cyrill Stachniss*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A post-processing pipeline that converts per-pixel Gaussians from feed-forward 3D Gaussian splatting into a compact, content-adaptive Gaussian set using adaptive superpixel clustering, latent representations, cross-view merging, and a level-of-detail decoder, achieving ~20x reduction with similar quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce redundancy and improve efficiency of per-pixel Gaussian splatting while preserving visual quality, enabling robust, back-bone-agnostic post-processing for multiple feed-forward methods.

Method: 1) Group spatially coherent Gaussians via adaptive superpixel segmentation guided by saliency to form clusters (fine in textured, coarse in homogeneous regions). 2) For each cluster, compress into latent representation via learned encoder. 3) Across views, match/merge latent representations using geometric overlap and feature similarity via a learned merger. 4) Use a level-of-detail decoder to generate final Gaussians at adjustable resolution; output is a compact representation ready for rendering. 5) Works as a post-processing module, backbone-agnostic.

Result: In experiments, achieves similar visual quality with only about 1/20th the number of Gaussians compared to per-pixel methods; more robust than prior primitive-count reduction methods; flexible quality-efficiency trade-off at inference.

Conclusion: The proposed structure-aware merging pipeline provides a robust, compact, and flexible alternative to reduce Gaussian splats while maintaining render quality, indicative of practical gains for 3D reconstruction/rendering pipelines.

Abstract: 3D scene reconstruction, modeling, and rendering are highly relevant for numerous tasks, and 3D Gaussian splatting has become a standard choice in this context. Its feed-forward variants provide fast reconstruction from sparse input views but often produce per-pixel primitives, leading to highly redundant and thus inefficient representations. We present a structure-aware merging pipeline that takes per-pixel primitives from any feed-forward method and consolidates them into a compact, content-adaptive Gaussian set while largely retaining visual quality at just $\frac{1}{20}^\text{th}$ of the Gaussians of a per-pixel method. We group spatially coherent Gaussians of similar appearance into variable-size clusters via adaptive superpixel segmentation guided by a saliency map, which allocates fine segments to textured regions and coarse segments to homogeneous areas. We compress each cluster into a compact latent representation through a learned encoder, then match and consolidate representations across views based on geometric overlap and feature similarity via a learned merger. A level-of-detail decoder then produces the final Gaussians at a controllable resolution, enabling a flexible quality-efficiency trade-off at inference. As a post-processing module, the pipeline is backbone-agnostic, leveraging the strengths of existing feed-forward methods. This leads to better and more robust quality than achieved by previous approaches that target a reduction in primitive count, while providing a highly compact representation, that can be rendered efficiently.

</details>


### [58] [Grid-Preserving Knowledge Distillation: Transferring Convolutional Inductive Bias to Vision Transformers under Data Scarcity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10723)
*Junyong Choi,Cheolhyeon Park,Jaehoon Cho*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: iBKD proposes training-time, grid-preserving distillation for ViTs to inherit CNN inductive biases without inference cost; uses Inductive Bias Attention Module to align student features to the teacher's grid via learned weights, channel and deformable spatial attention, and convolutional cross-attention across grids; yields state-of-the-art gains in data-scarce settings across diverse backbones and benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: ViTs underperform when data is scarce; CNN teachers help, but conventional distillation erases spatial inductive biases by pooling/flattening; need a distillation method that preserves the grid to maintain locality and translation equivariance.

Method: Introduce iBKD with Inductive Bias Attention Module that, during training, aggregates student layers onto teacher grid with learned weights; applies channel attention and deformable spatial attention to highlight structural cues; injects information via convolutional cross-attention operating between grids, preserving grid structure; training-only, no inference overhead.

Result: Empirical evaluation on seven ViT backbones and six data-scarce benchmarks shows iBKD outperforms both locality-guided distillation and general knowledge distillation baselines; gains increase as data becomes scarcer.

Conclusion: Preserving the grid during distillation is beneficial for transferring inductive biases to ViTs in data-scarce regimes; iBKD provides a practical, training-only module that improves performance without impacting deployment.

Abstract: Vision Transformers underperform convolutional networks when training data is scarce, and distilling convolutional inductive biases from a CNN teacher is an effective remedy that leaves the deployed model unchanged. General-purpose feature distillation, however, transfers little in this setting. The pooling, flattening, and logit-space projections it inherits from CNN to CNN pipelines discard the spatial grid in which locality and translation equivariance are encoded, and unlike a convolutional student, a ViT cannot rebuild that structure on its own. In this paper, we propose iBKD, a distillation framework that preserves the grid along the entire transfer path. Its core module, the Inductive Bias Attention Module, aggregates every student layer onto the teacher grid with learned weights, sharpens structural cues with channel and deformable spatial attention, and injects them through convolutional cross-attention that operates between grids rather than between token sets. The module is used only during training, so the deployed model is an unmodified ViT with no inference overhead. Across seven Transformer backbones and six data-scarce benchmarks, iBKD outperforms both locality-guidance methods and general knowledge distillation baselines, and its margin widens as training data shrinks.

</details>


### [59] [InterPruner: Interactive Structured Pruning via Taylor-Implicit Criterion and Language-Prior Modulator for Multimodal Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10724)
*Qi Ming,Zihan Yang,Shaoguang Huang,Si Sun,Hanqing Zhang,Nanqing Liu,Jiahui Lv,Juan Fang,Aleksandra Pizurica*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Interactive structured pruning for RGB-IR detectors; TIC + MIRA + SPCA identify channel importance and prune 50% with minimal accuracy loss, achieving +0.6% mAP on FLIR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce cross-modal channel redundancy and computational overhead in RGB–Infrared detectors by leveraging cross-modal interactions and dynamic scene cues.

Method: TIC (Taylor-Implicit Criterion) uses high-order Taylor expansion and the implicit function theorem to quantify channel importance; MIRA (Modality Interaction Redundancy Analyzer) assesses redundant channels via mutual compensability across modalities; SPCA (Scene-Prior Channel Anchor) uses language priors as semantic anchors to evaluate channel-scene relevance for dynamic channel importance estimation.

Result: Experiments on RGB–IR detection benchmarks (e.g., FLIR) show that pruning 50% of channels yields negligible performance degradation and even yields a 0.6% mAP gain on FLIR.

Conclusion: Introduces the first interactive structured pruning framework for RGB–Infrared object detectors, demonstrating effective cross-modal channel pruning with minimal loss; code will be released on GitHub.

Abstract: Multimodal object detection proves effective in remote sensing, especially the RGB-Infrared paradigm. The parallel feature extractors provide rich multimodal information for robust detection, yet introduce substantial channel redundancy and computational overhead. Existing pruning methods can reduce channel redundancy, but they are designed for unimodal backbones, overlooking cross-modal interactions and dynamic scene-wise redundancy. In this paper, we propose InterPruner, the first interactive structured channel pruning framework for RGB-infrared object detectors. Specifically, we first derive a Taylor-Implicit Criterion(TIC) to quantify channel importance via high-order Taylor expansion and the implicit function theorem. Then, a Modality Interaction Redundancy Analyzer (MIRA) identifies redundant channels via mutual compensability assessment. Finally, a Scene-Prior Channel Anchor (SPCA) uses language priors as semantic anchors to measure channel-scene relevance for dynamic channel importance estimation. Cross-modality channel pruning for RGB-Infrared detection is yet unexplored. Extensive experiments on RGB-infrared object detection dataset demonstrate that InterPruner maintains high performance with negligible degradation. Specifically, it even achieves a 0.6% mAP increase on the FLIR dataset when pruning 50% of the channels. Code will be available on GitHub to facilitate future work.

</details>


### [60] [Rethinking LLM Verification: Evidence Structure, Uncertainty, and Selective Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10725)
*Uma Ranjan,Kunal Tilaganji,Aditya Koul,Anurag Mahipal,Dashpreet Singh,Hriday Rana,Manan Jain,Sidharth Gupta,Ajo Babu George,Vineeth Balasubramanian,Nagarajan Natarajan,Amit Sharma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A two-stage abstention-driven framework for medical hypothesis verification uses targeted ontology grounding triggered only when the model abstains, improving MCQ and hypothesis accuracy on MedReason and MedQA for GPT-5.5 and DeepSeek-R1. Abstention serves as an informative control signal rather than random noise, enabling knowledge-graph–level reasoning without explicit KG construction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs in medicine risk relying on shortcuts; abstention can gate selective, more reliable reasoning. Grounding via ontology only when uncertain aims to improve safety and reliability without sacrificing coverage.

Method: Stage 1: LLM answers MCQ with an abstain option. Stage 2: if abstain, apply targeted ontology grounding to refine the hypothesis. The grounding is invoked only on abstentions, avoiding full KG use during normal inference. Evaluated on MedReason and MedQA with GPT-5.5 (Azure API) and DeepSeek-R1.

Result: Question-level accuracy improved from 82.9% to 92.5% (+9.6 pp). Hypothesis-level accuracy improved from 92.0% to 96.2% (+4.2 pp). These gains observed across two frontier models and two datasets. Abstention correlates with lower confidence, supporting its role as a meaningful control signal. The approach achieves knowledge-graph–level performance without constructing an explicit KG.

Conclusion: Abstention can be repurposed as a targeted refinement trigger to achieve reliable, high-accuracy medical reasoning with selective grounding. The framework offers a practical path to safer LLM deployment in medicine by combining abstention with ontology-grounded verification without upfront KG construction.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) often rely on shortcuts rather than systematic reasoning, raising safety concerns in medical applications. Allowing models to abstain when uncertain improves reliability but introduces a coverage accuracy tradeoff. We propose a two-stage framework for medical hypothesis verification in multiple-choice settings that manages this tradeoff through targeted ontology grounding, applied only when the model abstains. We show that abstention is not random but reflects genuine uncertainty, with abstained predictions associated with lower confidence. Across two frontier models (GPT-5.5, accessed via the Azure OpenAI API, and DeepSeek-R1), the proposed framework improves question-level accuracy by 9.6 percentage points (82.9% to 92.5%) and hypothesis-level accuracy by 4.2 percentage points (92.0% to 96.2%). Our experiments conducted on MedReason and MedQA show that abstention can be repurposed as a control signal for selective reasoning refinement, achieving knowledge-graph-level performance without explicit knowledge graph construction.

</details>


### [61] [Beyond Pixels: From Video Priors to 4D Worlds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10744)
*Zihao Liu,Xiaolong Shen,Zhenglin Zhou,Ruijie Quan,Yi Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Latent-to-4D, a direct latent-to-4D generation framework that aligns a video latent with a pretrained 4D decoder's token grid, enabling reusable 4D generation across video diffusion transformers within the same VAE with minimal retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 4D generation is hampered by distribution mismatch and error propagation when using RGB-based reconstructions or tying to a single generator; a reusable latent-to-4D interface could enable flexible, generator-agnostic 4D prediction.

Method: Aligns a video latent with the 4D decoder's token grid and refines it via frame-wise and global spatiotemporal attention; trained on ~1k reconstruction clips; a single checkpoint transfers unchanged across multiple video diffusion transformers within the same VAE family.

Result: Outperforms matched same-latent Wan+4RC cascades in projection-based DINO-F1 by 2.88–3.45 points on Text4D-200 and by 5.81 points on I4D-200; also preferred by human raters for geometry, temporal stability, and overall quality.

Conclusion: Latent-to-4D provides a reusable, generalizable interface for 4D generation that decouples 4D prediction from any single generator and shows strong quantitative and perceptual performance across multiple video models within the same VAE family.

Abstract: 4D generation synthesizes dynamic 3D scenes from conditions such as text or images. Existing methods either reconstruct generated RGB videos with a separate 4D model or adapt a particular video generator to predict geometry directly. The former suffers from distribution mismatch and error propagation, whereas the latter ties 4D prediction to a specific generator and may require retraining when the generator or conditioning regime changes. We ask whether the final denoised latents of video models that share a variational autoencoder (VAE) can instead provide a reusable interface to explicit 4D prediction. Building on this insight, we introduce direct latent-to-4D generation and instantiate it as Latent-to-4D, which bypasses RGB by aligning a video latent with the token grid of a pretrained 4D decoder and refining it through frame-wise and global spatiotemporal attention. Trained on roughly 1K existing reconstruction clips, a single checkpoint transfers unchanged across multiple video diffusion transformers within the same VAE family. On Text4D-200 and I4D-200, Latent-to-4D surpasses matched same-latent Wan+4RC cascades in projection-based DINO-F1 by 2.88--3.45 and 5.81 points, respectively, while also being preferred by human raters for geometry, temporal stability, and overall quality.

</details>


### [62] [Where To Look? : Causal Tracing of Vision Encoders in VLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10758)
*Naren Kumar S,Tirth Bhatt,Mayank Singh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Causal tracing reveals that vision-language models rely on highly causal visual tokens that often lie outside the target region; this non-local causality persists across models and corruption settings, indicating that strong multimodal performance does not require spatially localized causal representations. The study also shows that removing appearance cues asks whether models preserve visual structure, finding that visual cues are used to understand structure. A causal framework is proposed to study how visual information is transformed, preserved, and used by modern V-L models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand what visual information actually drives answers in vision-language models, addressing the gap between perceived visual content and the model's reasoning, and to determine whether causal information is spatially localized.

Method: Apply causal tracing to vision-language models to identify tokens with high causal impact on outputs; extend analysis to larger models and various corruption settings; test whether models preserve visual structure when appearance cues are removed; analyze spatial localization of causality and the role of appearance cues; synthesize results into a causal framework.

Result: Causal tokens are often outside the target region; the pattern holds across models and corruption settings; strong multimodal performance does not imply spatially localized causal representations; when appearance cues are removed, models still rely on visual cues to understand structure.

Conclusion: There is a gap between seeing, using, and reasoning over visual structure. The work provides a causal framework for studying how visual information is transformed, preserved, and ultimately used by modern vision-language models.

Abstract: Vision-language models can describe an image with remarkable accuracy, yet a more fundamental question remains unanswered: what visual information actually drives their answers? In this work, we investigate this question through causal tracing, and we observe that highly causal vision tokens often lie outside the target region. Extending the analysis to larger vision-language models reveals a similar pattern across models and corruption settings, suggesting that strong multimodal performance does not necessarily imply spatially localized causal representations. We further investigate: can these models preserve visual structure when appearance cues are removed? and find that visual cues are exploited to understand visual structures. Together, our experiments expose a gap between seeing, using, and reasoning over visual structure, and provide a causal framework for studying how visual information is transformed, preserved, and ultimately used by modern vision-language models.

</details>


### [63] [FADE: From Passive Verification to Active Discovery in Counterfactual Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10764)
*Fufangchen Zhao,Jinhu Fu,Jiachen Lei,Jiahong Wu,Xiangxiang Chu,Danfeng Yan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FADE introduces an evidence-first, two-stage training framework for counterfactual video understanding that reduces reliance on textual cues by progressively removing guidance (fading-anchor RL) and grounds predictions in decisive visual anomalies. It includes a pipeline to convert MCQs into MCQ/OQA/captioning tasks and achieves state-of-the-art results on DualityVidQA-test and IPV-Bench, outperforming GPT-5.6 and showing strong transfer from MCQs to unconstrained tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MCQ-based benchmarks leak target events through questions and options, turning a challenging active discovery problem into a text-guided verification task. There is a need for models that can autonomously discover and explain visual evidence in counterfactual video settings.

Method: Two-stage training: (1) evidence-internalized supervised fine-tuning to ground predictions in visual anomalies; (2) fading-anchor reinforcement learning to progressively remove textual guidance, forcing independent evidence discovery. A pipeline converts existing MCQ datasets into aligned MCQ, open-ended QA, and captioning tasks without extra data. Baseline is Qwen3-VL-8B; evaluation on DualityVidQA-test and IPV-Bench.

Result: FADE achieves state-of-the-art strict paired scores across MCQ, OQA, and captioning tasks on DualityVidQA-test and IPV-Bench, outperforming GPT-5.6. When moving from constrained MCQs to unconstrained OQA and captioning, retention is 90.4% and 67.4%, respectively, substantially higher than GPT-5.6’s 48.1% and 30.7%.

Conclusion: A simple yet strong framework that serves as a solid baseline for unconstrained counterfactual video understanding, with robust cross-task performance and potential for broader application.

Abstract: Counterfactual video understanding evaluates whether models grasp physical and commonsense regularities. However, existing multiple-choice question (MCQ) benchmarks inadvertently leak target events through their questions and candidate options. This reduces the core challenge from active discovery to text-guided verification. In this paper, we present FADE, an effective training framework for counterfactual discovery and explanation. Our method is built on an evidence-first, two-stage training paradigm. First, evidence-internalized supervised fine-tuning grounds the model's predictions in decisive visual anomalies. Second, we apply a fading-anchor reinforcement learning strategy that progressively removes textual guidance, compelling the model to independently discover and explain evidence. To rigorously evaluate this capability, we also introduce an effective pipeline that converts existing MCQ datasets into aligned MCQ, open-ended question answering (OQA), and captioning tasks without requiring additional data curation. Our simple approach yields strong results. Using Qwen3-VL-8B as the baseline, FADE achieves state-of-the-art strict paired scores across all three tasks on DualityVidQA-test and IPV-Bench, outperforming GPT-5.6. In specific, when transitioning from constrained MCQs to unconstrained OQA and captioning, our model demonstrates remarkable robustness. Its performance retention is 90.4% and 67.4% on DualityVidQA-test-substantially higher than the 48.1% and 30.7% retained by GPT-5.6. We hope this simple framework can serve as a solid baseline for future research in unconstrained counterfactual video understanding.

</details>


### [64] [MVTrack: Ultrafast Appearance-Free Moving Object Tracking from Compressed Bitstreams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10790)
*Iñaki Erregue,Kamal Nasrollahi,Sergio Escalera*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MVTrack enables fast compressed-domain tracking by detecting motion-vector fields (MVDet) and associating them with MVLink, achieving competitive accuracy with far lower compute than RGB detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RGB-based detectors are computationally expensive for large-scale tracking; leveraging H.264 bitstreams could bypass pixel reconstruction and enable scalable surveillance.

Method: Introduce MVDet to operate directly on motion-vector fields extracted from compressed video and MVLink as a lightweight kinematic association module, forming the MVTrack tracker.

Result: On VIRAT, MVTrack outperforms YOLO26n while using ~60× fewer parameters, ~40× fewer FLOPs, and ~8.6× lower CPU latency.

Conclusion: Compressed video data alone can support accurate, scalable surveillance tracking without pixel-level reconstruction, reducing resource demands substantially.

Abstract: Deploying modern video trackers at scale is bottlenecked by the computational cost of RGB-based object detectors. To this end, we present MVTrack, an ultrafast tracker for moving objects that operates directly on H.264 bitstreams. MVTrack combines MVDet, a lightweight detector for motion vector fields, with MVLink, a minimalist kinematic association module. On VIRAT, MVTrack outperforms YOLO26n while using 60$\times$ fewer parameters, requiring 40$\times$ fewer FLOPs, and reducing CPU latency by 8.6$\times$. These results demonstrate that compressed video data alone can enable accurate and scalable surveillance tracking, thereby bypassing the need for pixel reconstruction.

</details>


### [65] [E$^3$mo-Bench: A Scalable Benchmark for Multimodal Evoked and Expressed Emotion Understanding via Bayesian Pairwise Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10796)
*Lancheng Gao,Ziheng Jia,Shengyan Li,Zixuan Xing,Jiarui Wang,Huiyu Duan,Xiongkuo Min*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces E^3mo-Bench, a scalable benchmark for multimodal emotion understanding (evoked and expressed) with 12,314 QA pairs across 2,524 videos, plus two novel components: Bayesian Pairwise Alignment for continuous VAD estimation and E^3mo-Score for aggregation across a five-model committee. Evaluates three tasks (emotion perception, open-vocabulary recognition, VAD assessment) and finds a performance skew between evoked and expressed emotion paradigms, highlighting gaps in fine-grained recognition and dimensional assessment for MLLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks largely treat expressed and evoked emotions separately or rely on coarse, incomplete affective labels. There is a need for scalable, continuous, and fine-grained affective evaluation to drive multimodal LLMs toward robust affective understanding.

Method: Data: 12,314 QA pairs covering 2,524 videos with predefined affective perspectives. Tasks: (1) emotion perception, (2) open-vocabulary recognition, (3) VAD assessment. Bayesian Pairwise Alignment aggregates sparse pairwise judgments into anchor-referenced VAD estimates for reliable continuous labels. E^3mo-Score is a training-free agent that aggregates judgments from a five-model committee to improve VAD estimation.

Result: Extensive experiments validate effectiveness of the proposed framework. Findings reveal a pronounced performance skew between evoked and expressed emotion paradigms and expose MLLMs’ persistent deficits in fine-grained recognition and dimensional assessment.

Conclusion: The framework provides a scalable, robust benchmark and analysis pipeline, advancing the study of multimodal emotional intelligence by clarifying differences between evoked and expressed emotions and guiding future improvements in fine-grained, dimensional affect understanding.

Abstract: Understanding both expressed and evoked emotions is critical for multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to achieve comprehensive affect-aware interactions. However, existing benchmarks typically examine expressed and evoked emotions in isolation or are constrained to coarse-grained and incomplete affective characterizations. To bridge this gap, we introduce E$^3$mo-Bench, a scalable benchmark comprising $12{,}314$ question-answer pairs across $2{,}524$ videos with predefined affective perspectives. It evaluates evoked and expressed emotion understanding via $3$ complementary tasks: emotion perception, open-vocabulary recognition, and valence-arousal-dominance (VAD) assessment. To efficiently scale reliable continuous annotations, we propose Bayesian Pairwise Alignment, which aggregates sparse, low-burden pairwise judgments into anchor-referenced VAD estimates. Furthermore, we develop E$^3$mo-Score, a training-free agent that aggregates complementary judgments from a five-model committee to improve VAD estimation. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of our framework and expose a pronounced performance skew between evoked and expressed emotion paradigms. These findings, coupled with MLLMs' persistent deficits in fine-grained recognition and dimensional assessment, chart a clear course for advancing multimodal emotional intelligence.

</details>


### [66] [Beyond Fixed Luminance: Towards Panchromatic and Orthochromatic Image Colorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10798)
*Swarnim Maheshwari,Syed Imam Ali,Vineeth N. Balasubramanian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A luminance-agnostic RGB colorization method framed as full-RGB image editing, trained with a mixed grayscale objective to handle both standard luminance and red-insensitive orthochromatic grayscale; shown to be competitive on standard data and robust to orthochromatic inputs with fewer artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome limitations of fixed-L luminance in Lab-based colorization and sensitivity to non-natural grayscale in historical orthochromatic photography, enabling reliable colorization across varied luminance formations.

Method: Formulate colorization as full-RGB image editing using a foundation image-editing model. Train with a mixed grayscale objective that includes standard luminance grayscale and a red-insensitive grayscale to bridge panchromatic and orthochromatic conditions.

Result: Competitive performance on standard grayscale inputs; substantially more robust under orthochromatic inputs; qualitative comparisons and a human study report fewer visible color artifacts.

Conclusion: RGB-based editing with mixed grayscale training improves robustness to luminance variations and unusual grayscale formation while maintaining standard-performance levels.

Abstract: Most image colorization systems operate in $Lab$ space by predicting chroma ($ab$) while preserving an input-derived luminance channel ($L$). While effective on standard benchmarks, this fixed-luminance design restricts brightness changes and becomes unreliable when grayscale formation deviates from natural-image luminance, as in historical orthochromatic photography. We propose a luminance-agnostic colorization framework that formulates colorization as full-RGB image editing using a foundation image-editing model. To bridge modern panchromatic and historical orthochromatic conditions, we introduce a mixed grayscale objective that trains the model under both standard luminance grayscale and a red-insensitive grayscale formation. Experiments on COCO, ImageNet, and a multi-instance benchmark show that our method is competitive on standard grayscale inputs and substantially more robust under orthochromatic inputs, with qualitative comparisons and a human study indicating fewer visible color artifacts.

</details>


### [67] [Evaluating Semantic and Spatial Guidance for Foundation Model Segmentation of Small-Scale PV in Remote Sensing Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10801)
*Roni Blushtein-Livnon,Tal Svoray,Osher Rafaeli,Michael Dorman,Itay Fischhendler,Havazelet Yahel,Emir Galilee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Prompting strategy dominates SAM3-based PV segmentation in RS imagery; spatial guidance and hybrid prompting yield robust, data-efficient performance with limited labeled data across multiple datasets; textual prompting underperforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Off-grid PV adoption patterns require small, sparse targets; RS segmentation faces severe target-background imbalance; vision-language foundation models with prompts offer data-efficient mapping; unclear contributions of different prompt types; evaluate SAM3 prompting types under varying supervision, training, resolution, and imaging conditions across datasets.

Method: Systematic evaluation of SAM3 for small-scale PV segmentation in RS imagery; compare textual, geometric, and hybrid prompting; explore supervision levels, training strategies, spatial resolutions, and imaging conditions; use multi-temporal aerial imagery from a large off-grid rural region; validate across three additional datasets.

Result: Spatial guidance substantially improves segmentation accuracy and robustness; hybrid prompting achieves the highest accuracy and stability; textual prompting performs worst and is highly sensitive to supervision and imaging conditions; most gains occur with a few hundred annotated samples; transfer learning shows limited impact.

Conclusion: Prompting strategy is the key determinant of SAM3 adaptation, robustness, and generalization; promptable FMs have strong potential for scalable PV mapping in data-constrained off-grid regions.

Abstract: Spatio-temporal PV data are essential for understanding adoption processes in off-grid regions, yet such data remain largely unavailable. Automated segmentation of remote sensing (RS) imagery offers a promising solution; yet, residential PV systems remain challenging targets because of their small size and sparse distribution, resulting in severe target-background imbalance. Vision-language foundation models (FMs) provide a data-efficient paradigm through prompt-based semantic and spatial guidance, but the relative contribution of different prompt types remains unclear. We systematically evaluate SAM3 for small-scale PV segmentation in RS imagery by comparing textual, geometric, and hybrid prompting, under varying supervision levels, training strategies, spatial resolutions, and imaging conditions. Multi-temporal aerial imagery from a large off-grid rural region serves as a study site, with findings validated across three additional datasets. Prompting strategy emerged as the dominant factor governing model behavior. Textual prompting consistently produced the lowest performance and showed the greatest sensitivity to supervision and imaging conditions. In contrast, spatial guidance substantially improved both segmentation accuracy and robustness. Hybrid prompting achieved the highest accuracy and stability, indicating that semantic and spatial guidance provide complementary information. Most performance gains were achieved with only a few hundred annotated samples, demonstrating strong data efficiency. Transfer learning had limited overall impact, with only modest improvements observed for textual prompting under limited supervision. Overall, our findings establish prompting strategy as a key determinant of SAM3 adaptation, robustness, and generalization, highlighting the potential of promptable FMs for scalable PV mapping in data-constrained off-grid regions.

</details>


### [68] [BPG: Balancing Plasticity and Generalization for Domain Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10804)
*Qiang Wang,Songlin Dong,Shaokun Wang,Jizhou Han,Xiang Song,Chenhao Ding,Yuhang He,Yihong Gong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BPG introduces a dual-component framework for domain incremental learning: (1) BPG-Adapter dynamically scales domain-specific adapters based on feature separability, and (2) BPG-Inference uses a soft domain mixture to blend domain-specific models at test time, reducing domain-ID misselection and forgetting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Domain shifts cause performance drop; parameter-isolation DIL methods either underfit due to fixed adapters or overgrow due to redundant parameters. Dynamic, data-driven adaptation is needed to balance learning capacity and efficiency while mitigating forgetting.

Method: BPG-Adapter determines each domain's adapter hidden dimension from domain-specific feature separability; BPG-Inference implements a soft domain mixture of multiple domain-specific models during testing to mitigate misselection and improve robustness.

Result: On DomainNet, CDDB, and CORe50, BPG outperforms uniform adapter-based and hard-domain-selection baselines, achieving state-of-the-art average accuracy and reducing forgetting to as low as 0.22% on DomainNet.

Conclusion: BPG provides a unified, mechanism-driven approach to DIL that simultaneously adjusts adapter capacity per domain and robustly aggregates domain models during inference, yielding strong cross-domain generalization and minimal forgetting.

Abstract: Deep neural networks excel in various tasks but struggle to generalize across evolving data distributions, leading to significant performance degradation under domain shifts. Domain incremental learning (DIL) addresses this challenge by enabling models to continuously adapt while retaining prior knowledge. Among existing DIL approaches, the parameter-isolation paradigm achieves state-of-the-art performance. However, these methods often adopt a one-size-fits-all approach to adapt to new domains, resulting in either insufficient learning capacity or redundant parameters. In this work, we propose BPG, a unified framework that addresses both challenges through two complementary components: BPG-Adapter, which dynamically determines each domain's adapter hidden dimension based on domain-specific feature separability, and BPG-Inference, a soft domain mixture strategy that integrates multiple domain-specific models at test time, mitigating domain ID misselection. Experimental results on DomainNet, CDDB, and CORe50 demonstrate that BPG consistently outperforms uniform adapter-based approaches and hard domain selection strategies, achieving state-of-the-art average accuracy while reducing forgetting to as low as 0.22% on DomainNet.

</details>


### [69] [Fast and Memory-Efficient Wavelet Convolutions via I/O-Aware Reformulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10805)
*Amit Aflalo,Shahaf E. Finder,Roy Amoyal,Eran Treister,Oren Freifeld*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: An I/O-aware reformulation of WTConv substantially reduces memory traffic and speeds training: ~2.55x reduction in HBM traffic, up to 4.35x speedup, and about 50% lower peak memory, while preserving WTConv benefits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: WTConv expands receptive field with decomposition levels but causes severe data movement and memory-bound bottlenecks in high-bandwidth memory (HBM). A formal I/O model is needed to identify bottlenecks and guide optimizations that reduce data movement without sacrificing arithmetic efficiency.

Method: Develop an I/O model of WTConv and apply three algebraic reformulations: (1) recompute the Haar analysis butterfly on-chip to avoid off-chip data movement, (2) collapse the multi-level synthesis cascade into a single closed-form pass indexed by output-coordinate bits, and (3) fold learned per-channel scales into the convolution weights. Combine these into an I/O-aware fused implementation and evaluate WTConvNeXt across decomposition levels and tensor shapes.

Result: The reformulations substantially reduce modeled HBM traffic (≈2.55×) while maintaining comparable arithmetic. They achieve up to 4.35× training speedup over the reference WTConv and roughly halve peak memory usage, with the improvements observed across various decomposition levels and tensor shapes.

Conclusion: I/O-aware algebraic reformulations enable WTConv to retain its receptive-field benefits while drastically reducing execution time and memory footprint, removing prior systems overhead that limited practical efficiency.

Abstract: Wavelet convolution (WTConv) has emerged as an increasingly popular drop-in replacement for standard convolutions, expanding a network's receptive field exponentially with the number of decomposition levels while keeping the parameter count linear. However, its reference implementation is severely memory-bound due to excessive data movement through high-bandwidth memory (HBM). We develop an I/O model of WTConv to characterize this bottleneck and use it to guide three algebraic reformulations: (1) recomputing the inexpensive Haar analysis butterfly on chip, (2) collapsing the multi-level synthesis cascade into a single closed-form pass indexed by output-coordinate bits, and (3) folding learned per-channel scales into the convolution weights. Together, these reformulations enable an I/O-aware fused implementation that substantially reduces HBM traffic. We evaluate the WTConvNeXt configuration across decomposition levels and a broad range of tensor shapes. Despite performing comparable arithmetic, the reference WTConv is substantially slower than the depthwise convolution it replaces. Our reformulation reduces modeled HBM traffic by approximately $2.55\times$, yielding up to a $4.35\times$ training speedup over the reference while roughly halving peak memory usage. Thus, our reformulation preserves the benefits of WTConv while substantially reducing its execution time and memory footprint, removing the systems overhead that previously limited its practical efficiency.

</details>


### [70] [Modelling Geographic Atrophy Progression using Implicit Neural Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10807)
*Simone Sarrocco,Paul Friedrich,Florentin Bieder,Christina Bornberg,Philippe Valmaggia,Peter Maloca,Philippe Cattin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Implicit Neural Representations model GA progression in AMD from longitudinal FAF images, enabling past and future GA segmentation and FAF reconstruction; achieves lowest GA lesion MAE and highest DICE, with preserved image quality; code available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address highly individualized, data-scarce progression of late AMD GA; need a continuous, flexible representation to model progress over time.

Method: Train an INR to map coordinates and time to FAF intensities and GA segmentation; generate past and future timepoint predictions; compare against baselines across scenarios; evaluate with MAE of GA area and DICE; ensure FAF image fidelity.

Result: Competitive segmentation across scenarios; lowest GA lesion area MAE; highest DICE; FAF image quality preserved; demonstrated in low-data setting.

Conclusion: Implicit Neural Representations are effective for individualized GA progression modeling in low-data settings; potential for longitudinal prognosis; code released.

Abstract: Age-related Macular Degeneration (AMD) is the major cause of blindness in the Western world. Its late dry phase is characterised by irreversible atrophic areas, namely Geographic Atrophy (GA). Longitudinal Fundus Autofluorescence (FAF) image acquisitions are currently the main tool for assessing lesion growth over time at the image level. However, due to its highly individualised progression, the evolution of late AMD remains poorly understood. In this work, we propose using Implicit Neural Representations (INRs) to model GA progression at the individual level in a low-data setting. Our approach generates both FAF and GA segmentation at both past and future time points. Among the comparison models, our method achieves competitive segmentation quality across different scenarios, yielding the lowest Mean Absolute Error (MAE) for the GA lesion area and the highest DICE score, without sacrificing FAF image quality. The code is available at https://github.com/SimoneSarrocco/ga-progression-with-inrs.

</details>


### [71] [MIRA: Medical Image Reflection for Agentic Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10827)
*Shengzhi Wang,Jun Yang,Kai Wu,Xiaozhong Ji,Yiwen Ye,Ziyang Chen,Mingliang Xiong,Wen Fang,Mingqing Liu,Mengyuan Xu,Miaoxuan Shan,Caiyan Liu,Bin He,Qingwen Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MIRA is a medical visual diagnostic framework that uses tool-assisted evidence search and reflective verification to improve autonomous diagnosis, achieving higher accuracy and safer tool use across nine benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uncontrolled tool use can introduce noisy or misleading evidence in medical AI. The work aims to ensure tool actions are necessary and that resulting evidence supports the current hypothesis by adding reflective verification.

Method: Two-stage training: (1) tool-augmented Monte Carlo Tree Search explores diagnostic hypotheses and jointly verifies visual grounding accuracy and semantic consistency to generate supervised fine-tuning trajectories; (2) reinforcement learning with online reflective principle evolution distills failure cases into candidate principles, retaining only those improving held-out rollout rewards. Tools include zooming, grounding, pointing, rotation, measurement, and web search, invoked dynamically with evaluation of relevance and consistency.

Result: On nine medical visual reasoning benchmarks, MIRA achieves an average score of 64.73, improving the Qwen3-VL-8B backbone by 7.44 points. Useful tool-use judgments rise from 56.2% to 73.8%, while harmful judgments drop from 8.9% to 1.6%. Qualitative analyses show re-examination of evidence and adaptation of tool-use strategy.

Conclusion: MIRA demonstrates improved reliability and effectiveness in autonomous medical image diagnosis through dynamic, reflective tool use and principled verification; a project page is provided.

Abstract: Medical visual agents can use tools to inspect images and retrieve external knowledge, but indiscriminate tool use may introduce noisy or misleading evidence. Reliable diagnosis therefore requires not only acquiring additional observations, but also verifying whether tool actions are necessary and whether the resulting evidence supports the current hypothesis. We introduce MIRA (Medical Image Reflection for Agentic Diagnosis), a medical visual diagnostic framework for autonomous evidence search and reflective verification. MIRA dynamically invokes image-processing operations, including zooming, grounding, pointing, rotation, and measurement, as well as web search, while evaluating the relevance and consistency of the acquired evidence. We develop MIRA through a two-stage training strategy. First, a tool-augmented Monte Carlo Tree Search data engine explores diverse diagnostic hypotheses and jointly verifies visual grounding accuracy and semantic consistency to construct supervised fine-tuning trajectories. Second, reinforcement learning further improves decision-making through online reflective principle evolution: failure cases are distilled into candidate principles, and only principles that improve held-out rollout rewards are retained. Across nine medical visual reasoning benchmarks, MIRA achieves an average score of 64.73, improving its Qwen3-VL-8B backbone by 7.44 points. It also increases useful tool-use judgments from 56.2% to 73.8% and reduces harmful judgments from 8.9% to 1.6%. Qualitative analyses show that MIRA can re-examine evidence, correct premature conclusions, and adapt its tool-use strategy. Project page: https://MIRA-VL.github.io/

</details>


### [72] [UniProbe: A Learnable Token-Level Hallucination Detector for Large VLMs using Multi-Structural Internal Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10835)
*Dvir Samuel,Guy Bar-Shalom,Fabrizio Frasca,Ethan Fetaya,Yftah Ziser,Gal Chechik,Haggai Maron*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniProbe is a lightweight detector that models the LVLM trace as a graph and uses alternating structure-aware modules to detect and mitigate hallucinations, achieving state-of-the-art token-level and object-level detection with reduced hallucinations during decoding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LVLMs frequently hallucinate content not supported by visual input. Existing detectors are expensive, rely on external verifiers, or discard spatial/temporal structure. A unified, light detector that captures spatial, relational, and sequential cues from a frozen LVLM can enable targeted, efficient intervention.

Method: UniProbe builds a directed graph over image patches, query tokens, and generated tokens to capture relational semantics via attention weights. It processes the trace with alternating modules: a Graph Neural Network (GNN) for relational evidence, a Vision Transformer (ViT) for 2-D visual geometry, and a GRU for the response order. Interleaving enables cross-evidence interaction. A streaming variant detects and resamples hallucinated tokens during generation, and a self-adaptation strategy aligns the detector with the LVLM’s own outputs.

Result: Across diverse LVLM backbones, UniProbe achieves state-of-the-art token-level and object-hallucination detection. During decoding, it reduces object hallucinations by up to 55% at 1.06× the latency of standard generation.

Conclusion: UniProbe provides a unified, efficient approach to hallucination detection and mitigation in LVLMs, delivering strong detection performance and tangible reductions in hallucinated content, with streaming and self-adaptation variants enhancing practicality.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) achieve impressive visual reasoning and dialogue capabilities, yet frequently hallucinate content unsupported by the visual input. Effective mitigation requires token-level localization, enabling targeted intervention without discarding the entire response. Existing detectors require expensive full-model fine-tuning, rely on external verifiers that ignore the model's generation process, or reduce internal signals to isolated features and hand-crafted statistics, discarding spatial, sequential, and relational structure. We introduce \textbf{UniProbe}, a lightweight, unified, learnable detector that models a frozen LVLM's heterogeneous computational trace from a single forward pass. UniProbe constructs a directed graph over image patches, query tokens, and generated tokens, with attention weights encoding their relations. It processes this trace with alternating structure-aware modules: a GNN for relational evidence, a ViT for 2-D visual geometry, and a GRU for response order. Interleaving them allows spatial, relational, and sequential evidence to interact throughout the detector. We further develop a streaming variant for hallucination-aware decoding, which detects and resamples hallucinated tokens during generation, and a self-adaptation strategy aligning the detector with the LVLM's own generations. Across diverse LVLM backbones, UniProbe achieves state-of-the-art token-level and object-hallucination detection. During decoding, it reduces object hallucinations by up to 55\% at $1.06\times$ the latency of standard generation.

</details>


### [73] [PolyLayout: Hierarchical VLM-Guided Layout Generation Beyond Rectangular Rooms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10838)
*Yutong Jiang,Zahra Atashgahi,Carlos Soto Garcia Delgado,Ruben Brokkelkamp,Davide Zanutto,Efşan Sökmen,Shahin Shahkarami*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A hybrid hierarchical framework for scalable, retail-oriented 3D room layout generation, combining furniture clustering/intra-zone placement, macro-routing via a vision-language model to anchor zones within polygonal boundaries, and rule-based micro-arrangement optimization under architectural constraints, enabling clutter-free, irregular-topology layouts with low latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addresses the disconnect between academic furniture-placement methods and real-world retail needs by handling non-rectangular geometries and strict door/window constraints, enabling scalable spatial planning for production catalogs and irregular homes.

Method: A three-stage pipeline: (1) functional furniture clustering and fine-grained intra-zone placement; (2) macro-routing guided by a vision-language model to anchor clustered zones and standalone furniture within diverse polygonal boundaries; and (3) rule-based optimization for collision-free micro-arrangements that respect architectural constraints.

Result: Evaluated on production-scale catalogs and irregular real-world topologies; achieves the highest perceptual plausibility with good geometric compliance at low latency and extends to irregular boundaries not supported by existing methods.

Conclusion: The framework effectively bridges research and practice for retail spatial planning, offering scalable, geometry-aware layout generation that accommodates irregular boundaries with efficient performance.

Abstract: Generating physically plausible 3D room layouts is essential for home furnishing retail, enabling customers to visualize products in their own homes and confidently make purchasing decisions. However, a gap exists between academic research and real-world application: existing solutions primarily focus on algorithmic strategies for furniture placement, largely neglecting the non-rectangular geometries and strict door/window constraints prevalent in real homes. To bridge the gap, we introduce a hybrid, hierarchical framework tailored for retail, specifically designed to support scalable spatial planning applications. Our system decouples generation into three stages: (1) functional furniture clustering and fine-grained intra-zone placement; (2) macro-routing guided by a vision-language model (VLM) to anchor both these clustered zones and any remaining standalone furniture within diverse polygonal boundaries; and (3) rule-based optimization for collision-free micro-arrangements that respect architectural constraints. We evaluate our system on production-scale catalogs and a representative set of irregular real-world topologies. Our results show that our approach attains the highest perceptual plausibility while maintaining good geometric compliance at relatively low latency, and extends to irregular boundaries that existing methods do not natively support.

</details>


### [74] [The GENEA Challenge 2026: A Large-Scale Disentangled Evaluation of Speech-Driven Gesture Generation on the Seamless Interaction Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10839)
*Rajmund Nagy,Silvia Arellano García,Hendric Voss,Mihail Tsakov,Taras Kucherenko,Youngwoo Yoon,Gustav Eje Henter*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GENEA Challenge 4 evaluates five speech-driven gesture-generation systems via four large-scale studies on the Seamless Interaction dataset, introducing a semantic-gesture task and text-mismatch evaluation. Results reveal strong human motion realism but weak alignment, dyadic responsiveness, and semantic expressiveness, highlighting substantial gaps between human data quality and current models; data and code will be released for reproducibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To benchmark and dissect gesture-generation systems along independent axes (motion quality, speech alignment, social interactivity, and semantic expressiveness) using disentangled evaluation and mismatching paradigms, thereby identifying core bottlenecks and driving methodological improvements.

Method: Four large-scale user studies (n≈869 participants, 23k votes) evaluating five teams’ systems trained on the Seamless Interaction dataset. Methodologies include disentangled evaluation of motion quality and speech alignment, a dyadic mismatching study, and new semantic-text comparisons using the Grounded Gestures subset. Tasks include motion realism, speech-alignment, dyadic interactivity, and semantic mismatching evaluations.

Result: Motion realism: dataset-filtered segments outperform submissions (68–95% pairwise win rates). Speech alignment: a conceptual ceiling of 62% alignment; top submission at 32%; others near the chance level (~0%). Dyadic interactivity: ceiling ~65% appropriateness, but no system above chance. Semantic mismatching: humans correctly identify matching transcripts 79% of the time, yet best performing submission achieves only 8% appropriateness. Release of data and results for reproducibility.

Conclusion: Current systems lag behind human motion realism, especially in reacting to interlocutors and semantically expressive gestures. The disentangled and mismatching evaluation framework reveals critical gaps between perceptual realism and true interactivity, underscoring the need for models that listen, respond, and semantically align gestures with speech. Data and evaluation tooling will be publicly released to foster reproducibility and further research.

Abstract: This preprint presents the results of the fourth GENEA Challenge, a large-scale human evaluation of five speech-driven gesture-generation systems trained by participating teams on the Seamless Interaction dataset of dyadic conversations. As in the 2023 GENEA Challenge, we used a disentangled evaluation methodology to assess motion quality and speech alignment without confounding between the two, and performed a dyadic mismatching study to isolate the effect of listening and reacting to the interlocutor. We additionally introduce a new semantic gesture-generation task and a text-mismatching evaluation methodology using the Grounded Gestures subset of the data. In total, we ran four large-scale user studies, collecting over 23,000 votes from 869 test-takers.
  In the motion-realism study, the dataset's filtered segments had substantially higher motion quality than all challenge submissions (68-95% pairwise winrate). In the speech-alignment study, the motion-capture segments provided a conceptual ceiling at 62% alignment score, with the top submission significantly behind at 32% and the rest only slightly above the 0% expected of an input-independent system. In the dyadic study, motion capture again set the ceiling at 65% appropriateness score, but no submission scored substantially above chance, indicating that the systems could not yet respond to the interlocutor. Finally, the semantic mismatching evaluation found highly expressive gestures in the dataset (test-takers identified the matching transcript 79% of the time), yet almost all submissions failed to generate semantically expressive motion, with the best achieving only an 8% appropriateness score.
  The collected votes and outputs will be made publicly available at https://genea-workshop.github.io/2026/challenge/ to facilitate reproducibility and further research.

</details>


### [75] [Multi-View Relational Distillation for Spatial Reasoning with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10864)
*Kiet T. Nguyen,Hanbo Shim,Jinwoo Kim,Seunghoon Hong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A parameter-efficient distillation approach that transfers geometric relations via multi-view patch-wise cosine similarities, improving spatial understanding in VLMs without hurting language alignment, and generalizing to 3D tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLMs exhibit geometrically fragile visual-spatial representations, hurting spatial reasoning. Prior geometry grounding methods either risk spurious visual features via fine-tuning or incur large model size via feature fusion. Knowledge distillation can help but often disrupts visual–text alignment when matching teacher features.

Method: Distill patch-wise cosine similarities across multiple views (multi-view relational distillation) rather than direct teacher features. The student learns to preserve pretrained language–vision alignment while matching geometric relational cues across views, using far fewer added parameters and lower latency than feature fusion.

Result: MVRD improves visual-spatial reasoning across representative VLMs, outperforming supervised fine-tuning and feature distillation, and approaching feature fusion methods with significantly fewer parameters and lower latency. It yields more geometric visual representations while retaining language alignment and generalizes to 3D tasks such as object grounding, dense captioning, and visual QA.

Conclusion: Relational distillation across views provides an effective, lightweight path to grounding geometry in VLMs, maintaining language alignment while enhancing geometric understanding and enabling 3D scene tasks.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) have achieved strong image and video understanding, yet their visual-spatial representations remain geometrically fragile, leading to failures in spatial reasoning needed for embodied AI, robotics, and autonomous driving. Prior approaches to geometry grounding either fine-tune VLMs on spatial question answering, which can perpetuate spurious visual representations, or fuse features from large geometry-grounded vision models, which substantially increases model size at inference. Knowledge distillation from geometry-grounded vision models offers an alternative, but directly matching multi-view teacher features can disrupt the pretrained alignment between visual and textual representations, degrading object- and language-semantic capabilities. We propose multi-view relational distillation (MVRD), which distills patch-wise cosine similarities across views instead of the teacher features themselves. These relations encode geometric correspondences adequate for spatial understanding, while leaving the student representation underdetermined, allowing it to remain close to its pretrained vision- language space. Across representative VLMs, MVRD improves visual-spatial reasoning, outperforming supervised fine-tuning and feature distillation while approaching feature fusion methods with considerably fewer added parameters and lower latency. We show that MVRD makes visual representations more geometric while retaining language alignment, and generalizes to 3D scene understanding tasks such as object grounding, dense captioning, and question answering.

</details>


### [76] [NullEdit: Stealthy Image Protection via VLM Condition Redirection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10870)
*Weiyao Huang,Liqin Wang,Ziqi Sheng,Wei Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NullEdit is a stealthy no-op defense against vision-language model–driven image edits, suppressing edits without artifacts by redirecting the joint VLM representation and using cross-prompt gradient averaging; achieves a notable average EditReward IF reduction of 0.813 while preserving identity on CelebA-HQ/VGGFace2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prevent unauthorized manipulation of publicly released images edited via VLM-guided diffusion, without revealing the defense or introducing conspicuous artifacts.

Method: Target the joint VLM representation formed by the reference image and instruction before DiT conditioning; employ normal-edit and no-edit anchors to redirect this representation; use cross-prompt gradient averaging to transfer protection to held-out instructions; evaluate on Step1X-Edit and Qwen-Image-Edit across CelebA-HQ and VGGFace2.

Result: NullEdit reduces the EditReward IF score by 0.813 on average versus the SOTA baseline; preserves subject identity and source content.

Conclusion: NullEdit offers a stealthy, harmless no-op defense that prevents harmful edits at inference time without conspicuous artifacts or identity changes, demonstrated on standard face-image benchmarks; applicability and generalization to broader edits require further study.

Abstract: Modern image editors combine vision-language models (VLMs) with diffusion transformer backbones to modify a single reference image according to instructions without fine-tuning. This capability also enables unauthorized manipulation of publicly released images. Existing inference-time defenses either invalidate edits through conspicuous corruption, thereby exposing the protection, or allow them to proceed with identity or reference content drift, thereby failing to prevent the editing behavior itself. We instead target a stealthy and harmless no-op in which the requested edit is suppressed, the output remains natural and source-preserving without conspicuous artifacts or identity replacement, and harmful semantics requested by malicious instructions are absent. We propose NullEdit, which targets the VLM representation jointly formed from the reference image and instruction before it conditions the downstream DiT backbone. Using normal-edit and no-edit anchors, NullEdit redirects this representation, while cross-prompt gradient averaging transfers protection to held out instructions. Across Step1X-Edit and Qwen-Image-Edit on CelebA-HQ and VGGFace2, NullEdit reduces the EditReward IF score by 0.813 on average relative to the SOTA baseline while preserving subject identity and source content.

</details>


### [77] [ConfTriage: A Calibration-Aware LLM Triage Framework for Pulmonary Nodule Malignancy with Selective Specialist Deferral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10885)
*Md Rabiul Islam,Samir Abdaljalil,Erchin Serpedin,Hasan Kurban*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A generalist LLM can triage pulmonary nodule malignancy using natural-language radiologic descriptions, with a calibration layer and a specialist DL backstop; achieves high performance and partial automation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explore whether large language models can reduce reliance on large annotated imaging datasets by acting as a calibrated triage layer that defers uncertain cases to specialist DL models.

Method: ConfTriage framework: use language as the modality, calibration as a safety mechanism, and a selective DL backstop for low-confidence cases; provides finite-sample error bounds and an oracle inequality; ablation across five frontier LLMs on LIDC-IDRI; seven-way input ablation; zero-shot LLM inference to resolve cases, with uncertain cases sent to a specialist DL.

Result: F1 88.22%, AUC 0.92; 76.5% of cases resolved by zero-shot LLM inference alone; only uncertain cases referred to the specialist DL backstop; natural-language radiological descriptions dominate diagnostic signal; low-level image statistics are largely non-informative.
